2. MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Preparation - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the inoperative
Portable Fire Extinguishers.
B. References
Reference Title
26-26-01-020-801 Halon Fire Extinguisher Removal (P/B 401)
26-26-02-000-801 Water Fire Extinguisher Removal (P/B 401)
C. Deactivation of Inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers
SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-008
(1) Do the applicable task to remove the inoperative portable fire extinguisher: Halon Fire
Extinguisher Removal, TASK 26-26-01-020-801 or Water Fire Extinguisher Removal,
TASK 26-26-02-000-801.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-032
(2) Stow the extinguisher.
(a) Put the extinguisher in a bag
(b) Make sure that the extinguisher can not be used by mistake
NOTE: The extinguisher will be removed from the airplane at the next available
maintenance base.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-610-001
(3) Make sure that the necessary supply of serviceable extinguishers is available during
maintenance.
26-00-00-440-812MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Restoration - Portable Fire Extinguishers InoperativePDF p. 28
Open original AMM PDF - page 28 ↗ 
3. MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Restoration - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the inoperative
Portable Fire Extinguishers.
B. References
Reference Title
26-26-01-430-801 Halon Fire Extinguisher Installation (P/B 401)
26-26-02-400-801 Water Fire Extinguisher Installation (P/B 401)
C. Reactivation of the Inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers
SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-009
(1) Remove the inoperative extinguisher from the airplane.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-420-006
(2) Do the applicable task to install the new portable fire extinguisher Halon Fire Extinguisher
Installation, TASK 26-26-01-430-801 or Water Fire Extinguisher Installation,
TASK 26-26-02-400-801.
26-00-00-040-802MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Preparation - Wheel Well Fire Detection System InoperativePDF p. 29
Open original AMM PDF - page 29 ↗ 
4. MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Preparation - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps that prepare the airplane for flight with the Wheel Well
Fire Detection System Inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401)
22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401)
26-18-00-710-801 Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection
System - Operational Test (P/B 501)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left
134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Wheel Well Fire Detection System Deactivation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-210-001
(1) Just prior to engine start, check that brakes are cool per accepted procedure.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-740-001
(2) Deactivate the wheel well fire detection system, use one of the two methods.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-034
(3) Method 1: deactivate WHEEL WELL light and wheel well fire detection system input to fire
warning system.
(a) Remove the two light bulbs from the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel.
1) Install an INOP - FIRE BELL DISCONNECTED placard on the light switch.
(b) Disconnect the fire bell, do these steps:
1) Remove Flight Control Computer (FCC) B to get access to the wiring for the fire bell
(Flight Control Computer Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801).
2) Disconnect the wires from D742, pin 16 and pin 25 on the E1-4 shelf.
3) Cap and stow the wires.
4) Install the FCC B (Flight Control Computer Installation, TASK 22-11-33-400-801).
(c) Install a WHEEL WELL WARNING LIGHT AND BELL INOP placard on the captain's and
first officer's FIRE WARN lights, on the P7 panel.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-035
(4) Method 2: deactivate the detection elements of the wheel well fire detection system.
(a) Disconnect the terminal lugs A and B or connectors on both sides of the M270 detection
elements.
(b) Do this task: Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection System -
Operational Test, TASK 26-18-00-710-801.
1) Make sure that all tests pass except for the wheel well fire function with the disabled
element.
26-00-00
(c) Stow the wiring and terminal lugs on either side of M270 detection elements.

5. MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Restoration - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Wheel Well Fire
Detection Systems Inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401)
22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left
134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Wheel Well Fire Detection Systems Activation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-810-002
(1) Correct the fault.
(a) Find the fault code or description of the fault that occurred.
(b) Go to the applicable index or list in the FIM and find the FIM task number.
(c) Go to the task in the FIM and do the steps in the task.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-004
(2) Install the two light bulbs in the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel.
(a) Remove an INOP - FIRE BELL DISCONNECTED placard from the switch.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-010-008
(3) Remove Flight Control Computer - B to get access to the wiring for the fire bell (Flight Control
Computer Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801).
SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-004
(4) Install the wires from D742, pin 16 and pin 25 on the E1-4 shelf.
(a) Remove the WHEEL WELL WARNING LIGHT AND BELL INOP placard from the
captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-007
(5) Install Flight Control Computer - B (Flight Control Computer Installation,
TASK 22-11-33-400-801).
SUBTASK 26-00-00-420-007
(6) If it is necessary, install the terminal lugs A and B or connectors on both sides of the M270
detection elements.

6. MMEL 26-9 (DDPG) Preparation - Engine/APU Fire Extinguisher Test System (EXT TEST) (Squib
Test) Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the
engine/APU squib test (EXT TEST switch) inoperative.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Engine/APU Extinguisher System (EXT TEST) (Squib Test) Deactivation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-760-001
WARNING
USE THE CORRECT LAMP WHEN YOU MAKE THE SQUIB TESTER. IF YOU
USE AN INCORRECT LAMP , THE HIGH ELECTRICAL CURRENT CAN CAUSE
THE SQUIB TO FIRE. THIS WILL RELEASE THE EXTINGUISHING AGENT .
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(1) Prepare to do a test of the squib circuits:
(a) Make a squib test lamp with pigtail leads that end in pin contacts. Use only a 28 volt, 40
milliamp lamp in a applicable holder.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-036
(2) For the engines:
(a) Open these circuit breakers:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT
B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT
B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R
B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L
(b) Pull the engine 1 and engine 2 fire handles, on the P8-1 panel.
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU OPEN THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL.
THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL CONTAINS HIGH VOLTAGES AND
CURRENTS THAT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(c) Loosen the screws to lower the P6-2 circuit breaker panel.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL ARE CLEAR OF THE ENGINES
AND APU. ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING
BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
(d) Connect the test lamp across the circuit breaker shown in the table below.
(e) Turn the applicable fire handle in the direction that is shown in the table.
1) Make sure that the lamp comes on for each position.
NOTE: If the lamp comes on, the squib is satisfactory.

(f) Remove the test lamp after you do the check for all of the circuit breakers.
(g) Push the engine 1 and engine 2 fire handles, on the P8-1 panel, to the normal position.
(h) Close the P6-2 panel and install the screws.
(i) Close these circuit breakers:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name

(3) For the APU:
(a) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU
(b) Pull the APU fire handles on the P8-1 panel.
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU OPEN THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL.
THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL CONTAINS HIGH VOLTAGES AND
CURRENTS THAT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(c) Loosen the screws to lower the P6-2 circuit breaker panel.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL ARE CLEAR OF THE ENGINES
AND APU. ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING
BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
(d) Connect the test lamp across the circuit breaker shown in the table below.
(e) Turn the applicable fire handle in the direction that is shown in the table.
1) Make sure that the lamp comes on for each position.
NOTE: If the lamp comes on, the squib is satisfactory.

SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-005

8. MMEL 26-15 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Fire Extinguisher System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory
Fire Extinguisher System inoperative.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
200 Upper Half of Fuselage
C. Lavatory Deactivation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-006
(1) If the Lavatory Smoke Detector operates, do this step.
(a) Install INOP placard on the fire extinguisher bottle.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-007
(2) If the Lavatory Smoke Detector does not operate, do these steps.
(a) Empty the lavatory waste receptacle.
(b) Close and lock the lavatory door.
(c) Install an INOPERATIVE - DO NOT ENTER placard on the lavatory door.

10. MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory
Smoke Detector System inoperative.
26-00-00
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
200 Upper Half of Fuselage
C. Lavatory Deactivation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-010
(1) Do the following steps:
(a) Empty the lavatory waste receptacle.
(b) If necessary, disconnect the smoke detector.
1) Open the applicable LAVATORY SMOKE Circuit Breaker (CB).
a) Lock with a DO NOT CLOSE tag.
2) Remove the smoke detector.
3) Disconnect, cap and stow the electrical connector from the smoke detector.
4) Install the smoke detector.
5) Close the applicable LAVATORY SMOKE CB.
(c) Close and lock the lavatory door.
(d) Install an INOPERATIVE - DO NOT ENTER placard on the lavatory door.
26-00-00-440-804MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Restoration - Lavatory Smoke Detector System InoperativePDF p. 35

12. MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector Test Switch Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory
Smoke Detector Test Switch inoperative.
NOTE: This procedure is applicable to the inoperable SELF TEST switch, on the smoke
detector, or the TEST switch, on the Flight Attendant's Panel.
B. References
Reference Title
26-14-00-730-801 Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (P/B 501)
26-14-00-730-802 Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (P/B 501)
C. Lavatory Smoke Detector Deactivation
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-033
(1) For each smoke detector, do the lavatory smoke detection system test (Lavatory Smoke
Detection - Smoke Test, TASK 26-14-00-730-801 or Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test,
TASK 26-14-00-730-802).
(a) The test switch may be inoperative provided that each lavatory smoke detector is verified
to pass the smoke test.

14. MMEL 26-18 (DDPG) Preparation - Wing Body Overheat Test System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Wing Body
Overheat Test System inoperative.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
131 Center Section Wing Box, Body Station 540.00 to Body Station 663.75 - Left
26-00-00
(Continued)
Zone Area
132 Center Section Wing Box, Body Station 540.00 to Body Station 663.75 - Right
133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left
134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Check the Wing-Body Overheat Test System
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-012
(1) Apply heat from an appropriate heat source to an operable detector element in the left
wing-body overheat detection system and in the right wing-body overheat detection system.
NOTE: The substitute test heat device must not produce heat greater than 450°F (232°C).
This temperature limit should be observed in event fuel vapors exist in the area.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-028
(2) When a detector element in the left system is heated, observe that these lights come on:
(a) Left WING-BODY OVERHEAT light on the Air Conditioning Panel (P5).
(b) The AIR COND light on the glareshield P7.
(c) The MASTER CAUTION lights.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-029
(3) When a detector element in the right system is heated, observe that these lights come on:
(a) Right WING-BODY OVERHEAT light on the Air Conditioning Panel (P5).
(b) The AIR COND light on the glareshield P7.
(c) The MASTER CAUTION lights.

RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued)
SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-024
(3) Do the steps that follow to deactivate Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 and prevent anomalies in the
indication of the DISCH light during operation and the EXT FWD and EXT AFT lights during the
system test:
NOTE: The steps that follow are only applicable to the Boeing System. For systems installed
by STC, see the STC holder.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-001
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIB. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE
CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY
AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(4) Disconnect the electrical connectors from the bottle.
(a) Before you touch the squibs, do the procedure for devices that are sensitive to
electrostatic discharge, these are the tasks:
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
(b) Disconnect, cap, and stow electrical connector D12816 on the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2
pressure switch.
(c) Disconnect electrical connectors D12820 and D12818 from the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2
squibs.
WARNING
PUT A PROTECTIVE COVER ON THE BOTTLE SQUIB. IF YOU DO NOT
PUT A PROTECTIVE COVER ON THE SQUIB, THE FIRE
EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY
AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
(d) Install a jumper across pins 1 and 2 on the electrical connectors D12820 and D12818.
(e) Cap and stow the connectors.
SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-002
(5) Close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
821 Forward Cargo Door
(a) Install the lining at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment (at the air conditioning
distribution bay).
SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-013
(6) Do the step that follow to INOP the Fire Extinguisher Bottle No. 2:
(a) Install the Fire Bottle No. 2 INOP Placard on the Cargo Fire Panel (P8).
SUBTASK 26-00-00-860-002
(7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2

26-10-00-710-801Fire and Overheat Detection System - Operational TestPDF p. 73
Open original AMM PDF - page 73 ↗ 
2. Fire and Overheat Detection System - Operational Test
(Figure 501 and Figure 502)
A. General
(1) The operational test makes sure that the Engine, Auxiliary Power Unit (APU), and
Compartment Fire/Overheat Detection Systems operate correctly. The operational test uses
only equipment that is installed on the airplane and it does not disturb the airplane systems.
The operational test consists of these tests:
(a) The FIRE/OVHT test for loops A and B.
(b) The FIRE/OVHT test for loop A.
(c) The FIRE/OVHT test for loop B.
(d) The FAULT/INOP test for loops A and B.
(e) The test for an Engine 1 Detection Fault.
(f) The test for an Engine 2 Detection Fault.
(g) The test for an APU Detection Fault.
(h) The Air Mode Test for the P28 APU Remote Control Panel.
(2) This test will do a check of all the systems affected by the detection test switches on the P8-1
engine and APU fire control panel. In other words, when you do the detection tests, you will
look for every indication that is affected by the test.
(3) A brief system description is included at the end of the procedure, refer to Table 501,
Table 502, Table 503.
(4) The Engine and APU Fire Detection Systems are monitored and controlled by the fire detection
control module, M279, in the electrical and electronics compartment. The Compartment
Overheat System is monitored and controlled by the compartment overheat detection
controller, M237, in the electrical and electronics compartment. The module and the controller
send fire, overheat, and fault signals to the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, and the P7
pilot's glareshield, in the flight compartment. The module also sends an APU fire signal to the
P28 APU remote control panel, in the right main wheel well.
(a) The Engine and APU Fire, Overheat, and Fault Warning Indications are as follows:
26-10-00
Table 501/26-10-00-993-805

FIRE WARN Lights (red) P7

(b) The Engine Fire Detection System has dual sensors in each loop and operates in "AND"
logic. Therefore, both loops must sense a fire or a fault before the fire or fault indication is
shown. The following table is a summary of the indications that will be given for the
various conditions of the sensing loops:

the NORMAL position, then to the FAULT INOP position, and then to the NORMAL
position:

26-10-00-700-801Overheat Detection Test Using Handheld LCRZ MeterPDF p. 89
Open original AMM PDF - page 89 ↗ 
3. Overheat Detection Test Using Handheld LCRZ Meter
(Figure 503)
A. General
(1) This task consists of the following procedures using a 1v/1kHz AC LCRZ impedance meter:
(a) Instructions for the health check of the compartment overheat loop detectors and wiring.
1) This procedure is used to detect shorts, open, and degraded overheat loop
detectors in the wing and body overheat detection system.
(b) Instructions for locating faulty detector or wiring for compartment overheat loop that reads
less than nominal range.
1) This procedure is used to isolate an entire loop to a degraded or failed detector, or
to a wiring problem.

E. Prepare for Overheat Detection Loop Health Check and Troubleshooting
SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-017
(1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.
NOTE: Electrical power must be off to prevent incorrect LCRZ meter readings because of
Electromagnetic Interference (EI).
SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-018
(2) Make sure that the battery switch is in the OFF position on the P5 overhead panel.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-019
(3) Make sure that all ground power is disconnected.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-020-001
(4) Remove the M237 compartment overheat detection control unit (TASK 26-18-01-000-801).
SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-001
(5) Install the overheat detection module extender box assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, on
the compartment overheat detection control unit mount.
(a) Do not connect the test box assembly to the extender box assembly with adapter cable
assembly.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-020-002
(6) If overheat detection module extender box assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, is not
available, remove the flight control computer B if it is necessary (TASK 22-11-33-000-801).
NOTE: Removal of flight control computer B is for easier access to overheat detection control
unit electrical connector.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-020
(7) Prepare the loop controller, COM-17158, or Inductance, Capacitance, Resistance and
Impedance (LCRZ) meter, COM-20443 (preferred), or loop controller, COM-17417, for the test.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE A NON-EQUIVALENT METER, OR A DC METER, FOR THE
LOOP ELEMENTS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE CAN OCCUR.
(a) If you use the loop controller, COM-17158, or Inductance, Capacitance, Resistance and
Impedance (LCRZ) meter, COM-20443 (preferred), do the following steps:
1) Connect the black test lead to the black receptacle port.
2) Connect the red test lead to the red receptacle port.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE A NON-EQUIVALENT METER, OR A DC METER, FOR THE
LOOP ELEMENTS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE CAN OCCUR.
(b) If you use the loop controller, COM-17417, do the following steps:
1) Connect the black test lead to the black receptacle port.
2) Connect the red test lead to the red receptacle port.
3) Connect the blue test lead to the blue receptacle port.

Table 504/26-10-00-993-808 Overheat Detection Loop Health Check
Compartment Overheat Detection Controller Receptacle

SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-001
(3) Prepare the loop controller, COM-17158.
(a) For each measurement do these steps:
1) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading.
2) Set the switch to TEST then back to ISOL to reset unit.
3) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading.
4) Make sure that both readings are similar to provide accurate results.
(b) Check for a short or degraded detector by taking the measurements per Table 504.
1) If a reading is greater than the expected reading, check the wiring between the
D742 connector and the first detector in the loop for an open circuit condition.
NOTE: A greater reading is permitted if the overheat detection unit BITE test
passes.
2) If a reading is between 5k ohms and the nominal range, a scheduled repeat test for
degradation or replacement of the detector is recommended at the operator's
convenience.
NOTE: This indicates that one of the detectors is degraded. The degraded detector
may continue to degrade resulting in intermittent failures until it completely
fails, which is typically within 180 days.
a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty
Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”.
26-10-00
3) If a reading is < 5k ohms, do the following to isolate the problem:
NOTE: This indicates that either one of the detectors is shorted between the center
conductor and ground, or that there is a problem in the wiring.
a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty
Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-080-001
(4) Disconnect the black test lead from ground.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-080-002
(5) Disconnect the black test lead from the loop controller, COM-17158, and connect to the green
receptacle port.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-006
(6) Connect the red test lead and green test lead to the breakout jacks on the front panel of the
overheat detection module extender box assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, as identified in
Table 505 (View C, Figure 503).
Table 505/26-10-00-993-809 Overheat Loop Detection Open Check
Compartment Overheat Detection Controller Receptacle
D742 Pin *[1] Expected Reading
2 to 8 < 15 ohms
4 to 12 < 15 ohms
9 to 29 < 15 ohms
10 to 11 < 20 ohms
13 to 27 < 20 ohms
21 to 24 < 15 ohms
*[1] Refer to WDM 26-12-11.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-002
(7) Set the loop controller, COM-17158, switch to CONT .
(a) For each measurement, do these steps:
1) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading.
2) Set the switch to TEST then back to CONT to reset unit.
3) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading.
4) Make sure that both readings are similar to provide accurate results.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-003
(8) Check for an open detector or circuit by taking the measurements per Table 505.
(a) If the reading is greater than the values shown in Table 505, do continuity checks of the
applicable loop segments to isolate the failed loop or wiring.
(b) Replace the faulty detector or repair wiring, as applicable.

SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-004
(3) Prepare the Inductance, Capacitance, Resistance and Impedance (LCRZ) meter, COM-20443.

1) Press the SEC/LEV button momentarily to toggle setting to 1V.
(e) Press the RATE/P-S button momentarily until “SLOW” is displayed for a more stable
reading.

1) Press the RATE/P-S button momentarily to toggle to PAL (parallel) mode.
(g) For each measurement allow reading to stabilize and record reading.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-005
(4) Check for a short or degraded detector by taking the measurements per Table 504.
(a) If a reading is greater than the expected reading, check the wiring between the D742
connector and the first detector in the loop for an open circuit condition.
NOTE: A greater reading is permitted if the overheat detection unit BITE test passes.
(b) If a reading is between 5k ohms and the nominal range, a scheduled repeat test for
degradation or replacement of the detector is recommended at the operator's
convenience.
NOTE: This indicates that one of the detectors is degraded. The degraded detector may
continue to degrade resulting in intermittent failures until it completely fails, which
is typically within 180 days.
1) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or
Wiring for Overheat Detection Loop”.
(c) If a reading is < 5k ohms, do the following to isolate the problem:
NOTE: This indicates that either one of the detectors is shorted between the center
conductor and ground, or that there is a problem in the wiring.
1) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or
Wiring for Overheat Detection Loop”.

(5) Disconnect the black test lead from ground.

I. Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-011
(1) For the applicable loop section, disconnect the loop at the approximate half way point (WDM
26-12-11).
NOTE: This will allow you to determine which side is faulty. For example, for the loop that
connects to pins 4 and 12 of connector D742, disconnect between detectors M1761
and M268.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-012
(2) Check the readings again at each pin at the rack connector.
(a) The side that shows the lower reading is the faulty side.
(b) The resistance values should roughly double from the original expected value.
NOTE: For example, if the original expected reading was 21k ohms ± 5k ohms and you
measured 5k ohms, when you disconnect the loops at the halfway point, the
resistance should be +42k ohms on each side on a good system. If one side
reads 42k ohms, and the other side reads 5k ohms, the faulty side is the one
reading 5k ohms.
1) Note the faulty side, and reconnect the loop segments that were disconnected in the
previous step.
2) Disconnect the loop at the approximate half way point of the faulty side.
3) Check the readings again at each pin at the connector rack.
a) The side that has the lower reading is the faulty side.
4) If it is necessary, continue to check to isolate to the faulty loop section.
5) Replace the faulty detector (TASK 26-18-02-000-801, TASK 26-18-02-400-801), or
repair wiring (Standard Wiring Practices Manual (SWPM)), as applicable.
NOTE: Value for individual loops is 100k ohms, but this number varies by length. A very long
section, 200+ inches, could read 100k ohms or below.
26-10-00
J. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

from the compartment overheat detection control unit mount.

(TASK 22-11-33-400-801).

4. Overheat Detection Test Using UN-ATE by Technologies Harness Scanner
A. General
(1) This task gives the instructions to do a health check of the compartment overheat loop
detectors and wiring using the wiring tester, COM-17989.
(a) This task is used to detect shorts, open, and degraded overheat loop detectors in the
wing and body overheat detection system.

SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-016
(2) Healthy loop - All measurements are within their tolerance.
(a) Once the scan completed, wiring tester, COM-17989, shows NO HIGHLIGHTon any loop
circuit indicating all loop circuit are HEALTHY .

SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-015
(3) Loop inner core measurement outside of tolerance.
(a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags an OUT OF TOLERANCE inner to inner core loop
circuit.
1) This problem may be an indication of the degrading wire/loop connection (corrosion,
pin insert, etc).
2) The procedure is to validate with conventional procedures and visual inspection.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-014
(4) Opened inner core loop fault location procedure.
(a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags an OPENED or discontinuity along the loop inner
core.
NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, fault detection feature is used to locate the
discontinuity point.
1) Select the problematic loop and select FIRELOOP DIAGNOSTIC and then
IDENTITY BAD LOOP to locate the closest point to the open fault location.
NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, diagnostic feature may take up to ten
minutes to process. Once the process is completed, the closest overheat
fireloop segment causing the fault is highlighted.
2) Confirm the damage part (wire or fireloop segment) that cause the problem, do
these steps:
a) Go to the fireloop segment located by the wiring tester, COM-17989, and do a
check for any visible damage that may cause the discontinuity in the loop.
b) Manually disconnect one side of the fireloop segment closest to the wiring
tester, COM-17989, located point.
c) Connect the INNER core lead at both sides of the connector to the ground
(aircraft chassis) using two short cables with alligator clips and lead adaptors.
NOTE: This location is the manually grounded point.
d) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software select REFRESH IMPEDANCE to
re-measure the three impedance measurements of the selected loop (inner to
inner core and inner to ground x 2).
e) The next step confirms if the fault is located at the RIGHTor LEFT side of the
previously grounded point (Table 508).
NOTE: Table locates the fault segment according to the measurement results.
26-10-00

disconnected fireloop segment.
g) Move to the next wire/fireloop segment (RIGHTor LEFT according to the
previous deduction) and disconnect one side of the that segment and connect
the inner core leads to the ground.
NOTE: This location is the second grounding point.
h) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software redo the measurement by selecting
REFRESH IMPEDANCE.
i) Confirm the side (RIGHTor LEFT) of the faulty segment.
NOTE: After this step, the faulty part may be precisely located. In some
cases, other disconnecting and grounding processes may be required,
especially in cases where multiple wire segments are inserted
between overheat fireloop segments.
j) Once the faulty part located, confirmed its condition using conventional Boeing
or operator procedure.
k) Replace or repair the part.
l) Do the complete scan in Step (1) again to confirm the repair.
SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-017
(5) Shorted bad loop fault location procedure.
(a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags a SHORTED loop or alteration of the loop insulation
quality.
NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, fault detection feature is used to locate the short
point.
(b) Select the problematic loop and select FIRELOOP DIAGNOSTIC and then IDENTIFY
BAD LOOP to locate the closest point to the short fault location.
NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, diagnostic feature may take up to ten minutes to
process. Once the process is completed, the closest wire/fireloop segment
causing the fault is highlighted.
(c) Confirm the damage part (wire or fireloop segment) that cause the problem, do these
steps:
1) Go to the wire/fireloop segment located by the wiring tester, COM-17989, and check
for any visible damage that may cause the short in the loop.
2) Manually disconnect one side of the fireloop segment closest to the wiring tester,
COM-17989, located point.
NOTE: This location is the floating point.
26-10-00
3) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software select REFRESH IMPEDANCE to
measure the three impedance measurements of the selected loop (inner to inner
core and inner to ground x 2).
4) The next step confirms if the fault is located at the RIGHTor LEFT side of the
previously floating point (Table 509).

26-11-00-710-801Engine Fire Detection - Operational TestPDF p. 117
Open original AMM PDF - page 117 ↗ 
2. Engine Fire Detection - Operational Test
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The operational test does a check of the engine fire detection system without disturbing the
detection system and it only uses the equipment that is on the airplane. This test will verify the
circuit from the P8-1 engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire control panel to the engine and
APU fire detection control module, M279. The test will make sure that the engine and APU fire
detection control module, that continuously monitors the detection loops, operates correctly.
B. References
Reference Title
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Prepare for the Operational Test
SUBTASK 26-11-00-860-001
(1) Make sure that the electrical power is supplied (TASK 24-22-00-860-811).
SUBTASK 26-11-00-860-002
(2) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2
A 23 C00403 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION APU
A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1
SUBTASK 26-11-00-860-003
(3) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
A 19 C00388 FIRE PROTECTION DET OVHT WW WING BODY
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-001
(4) Make sure that the two OVHT DET switches, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, in
the flight compartment are in the NORMAL positions.
26-11-00
E. FIRE/OVHT Test
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-002
(1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the
OVHT/FIRE position.
NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the wheel well and APU detection system and the
indications, on the engine and APU fire detection control module, M729, in the
electronic equipment bay.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) Engine 1 Fire Handle light (red).
2) Engine 2 Fire Handle light (red).
3) ENG 1 OVERHEAT light (amber).
4) ENG 2 OVERHEAT light (amber).
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red).
3) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
(c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, in the aural warning module, M315,
comes on.
RYR 372-999
(d) Make sure that the lights on the left and right engine start levers, on the P10 engine start
lever panel, comes on.
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-008
(2) Push the captain's or first officer's FIRE WARN switch.
NOTE: If you push the either of the FIRE WARN lights, on the P7 glareshield panel, or the
BELL CUTOUT switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, the same
conditions will occur. The FIRE WARN and BELL CUTOUT switches cause the same
resets but from different locations.
(a) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that this light goes off:
1) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red).
(b) Make sure that you do not hear the fire bell.
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-003
(3) Release the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights goes off:
1) Engine 1 Fire Handle light (red).
2) Engine 2 Fire Handle light (red).
3) ENG 1 OVERHEAT light (amber).
4) ENG 2 OVERHEAT light (amber).
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights goes off:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
26-11-00
RYR 372-999
(c) Make sure that the lights on the left and right engine start levers, on the P10 engine start
lever panel, turns off.
RYR ALL
F . FAULT/INOP Test
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-004
(1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the
FAULT/INOP position.
NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the APU detection system and the indications, on the
engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the electronic equipment bay.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the FAULT light (amber)
comes on.
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-005
(2) Release the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the FAULT light (amber) is
off.
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights are off:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
G. FAULT/INOP Test from the Engine and APU Fire Detection Control Module, M279
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-006
(1) Push and hold the FAULT/INOP TEST switch, on the engine and APU fire detection control
module, M279, in the electrical and electronics compartment.
NOTE: Ignore the indications for the APU detection system and the indications that will occur
in the flight compartment, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, and the P7
glareshield panel.
(a) On the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, make sure that these lights
comes on:
1) ENGINE 1 LOOP A (amber).
2) ENGINE 1 LOOP B (amber).
3) ENGINE 2 LOOP A (amber).
4) ENGINE 2 LOOP B (amber).
5) The three FAULT CODE lights are ON-ON-ON.
SUBTASK 26-11-00-710-007
(2) Release the FAULT/INOP TEST switch, on the engine and APU fire detection control module,
M279.
(a) On the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, make sure that these lights
are off:
1) ENGINE 1 LOOP A (amber).
26-11-00
2) ENGINE 1 LOOP B (amber).
3) ENGINE 2 LOOP A (amber).
4) ENGINE 2 LOOP B (amber).
5) The three FAULT CODE lights are OFF-OFF-OFF .
H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 26-11-00-860-004
(1) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
A 19 C00388 FIRE PROTECTION DET OVHT WW WING BODY

3. Engine Fire Detection Loop Resistance - System Test
(Figure 502)
A. General
(1) The loop resistance test does a check of the engine fire detection loop circuit. It disturbs the
engine fire detection system by removing the engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire
detection control module, M279, and it requires some additional test equipment.
B. References
Reference Title

(Figure 501, Figure 503)

engine fire detection system but it does require additional test equipment.

26-11-00

position.

panel, goes off.
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these conditions occur:
1) The captain and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights goes off.
2) The OVHT DET light goes off.

A. References
Reference Title

B. Location Zones
Zone Area
411 Engine 1 - Engine
421 Engine 2 - Engine
C. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 26-11-01-010-001
(1) To access the fan fire detector assemblies, do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels,
TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 26-11-01-010-002
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU
OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE
DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST
REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL
PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(2) To access the core fire detection procedure, do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser
(Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
26-11-01
D. Fire Detector Element Removal
SUBTASK 26-11-01-860-001
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name

A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2
A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1
SUBTASK 26-11-01-020-001
(2) To remove the element [6] from the upper fan fire detector assembly [1], do these steps
(Figure 401):
(a) Remove the nut [2] and washer [3] from the terminal.
1) Disconnect the leads [4] from the terminal.
(b) Loosen the bolt [8] on each applicable clamp [7].
(c) For the fire detector with the mounting strap [18], remove the four nuts [17], two strap
clamps [16], four washers [15], four screws [14], and mounting strap [18].
(d) For the fire detector with the two clamps [19], remove the four nuts [17], two strap
clamps [16], four washers [15], four screws [14], and two clamps [19].
(e) Remove the element [6] form the clamps [7].
CAUTION
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE OR INSTALL THE GROMMETS INTO
THE ELEMENTS OF THE FIRE DETECTOR FOR THE ENGINE. IF YOU
DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO THE ELEMENTS CAN OCCUR.
(f) Remove the grommets [9] from the element [6] to use on the new element.
(g) Remove the element [6].

F . Fire Detector Assembly Installation
SUBTASK 26-11-01-420-009
(1) Do these steps to install the upper fan fire detector assembly [1] (Figure 401):
(a) Make sure that the ends of the upper fan fire detector assembly [1] and brackets are
clean.
(b) Position the upper fan fire detector assembly [1] on the engine.
(c) Make sure that the preload between the upper fan fire detector assembly [1] and attach
points is not more than 10 lbf (44.48 N).
(d) Install the bolts [5] that hold the upper fan fire detector assembly [1] to the engine.
1) Tighten the bolts [5] to 78 in-lb (8.8 N·m) - 82 in-lb (9.3 N·m).
(e) Apply Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006, under the heads of the bolts [11].
(f) Install the bolts [11] at each applicable clamp [13] location along the support tube [10]
(View E, Figure 401).
1) Make sure that the sleeve [12] is installed.
2) Tighten the bolts [11] to 73 in-lb (8.25 N·m) - 77 in-lb (8.70 N·m).
(g) Make sure that there is at least 0.12 in. (3 mm) between the elements [6] and support
tube [10].
(h) For the top fire detector harness, MW0316, do these steps:
1) Achieve the minimum clearance of 0.15 in. (3.81 mm) between the fire detection
wires and strut insulation blanket, do the following steps:
26-11-01
a) Adjust the wires.
b) Secure per SWPM 20-10-11.
(i) For the top fire detector, install the leads [4] as follows (View B, Figure 401):
NOTE: If the leads are different sizes, install the lead with the small hole on top of the
large hole.
1) Install the leads [4], washer [3], and nut [2].
a) Make sure that the distance between the leads [4] is approximately 150
degrees.
b) Tighten the nut [2] to 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4 N·m).
(j) For the bottom fire detector, install the leads [4] as follows:
NOTE: If the leads are different sizes, install the lead with the small hole on top of the
large hole.
1) Install the leads [4], washer [3], and nut [2].
a) Make sure that the leads [4] are positioned approximately as shown (View F ,
Figure 401).
b) Tighten the nut [2] to 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4 N·m).

<a> Rotate the lead [26] up to 30 degrees counterclockwise from its

NOTE: The most effective range for the lead position to
minimize the ground wire loop and to prevent ground

clamp and 30 degrees counterclockwise from its parallel
position.

<a> Move the lead [26] counterclockwise in increments of 10 degrees
until the ground wire loop is minimized.
<b> If you observe the tension in the ground wire, do not continue to
move the lead [26].

C. Engine Fire Detector Inspection
SUBTASK 26-11-01-210-001
(1) Do a visual check of the fire detectors for nicks, cracks, cuts, burrs, corrosion, breaks, scoring,
deformation, dents, thread damage, or other defects.
(a) Make sure that the markings and nomenclature are complete and easy to read.
SUBTASK 26-11-01-211-001
(2) Make sure that the ceramic insulation around the terminal stud of the fire detectors is not
damaged.
NOTE: Refer to the manufacturer’s Component Maintenance Manual (CMM) for permitted
defects.
SUBTASK 26-11-01-211-002
(3) Do a visual check of the sensor element for abrasion, kinks, cuts, or severe pits.
NOTE: Chafing damage shows a clearance problem in the airplane installation.
NOTE: If the sensor element tube has no gas leak, small dents, nicks, or pits are permitted.
NOTE: The fire detector will fail the internal resistance test if there is a gas leak
SUBTASK 26-11-01-212-001
(4) Make sure that there is no contact between the forward transponder of the upper fan case
engine fire/overheat detector and strut insulation blanket (View F , Figure 601).
SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-001
(5) If there is a contact and wire insulation is damaged, repair the wire as described below and
change the routing of the wire (View F , Figure 601).
(a) If no contact is found and the wires are routed, no further action is required.
SUBTASK 26-11-01-030-001
(6) Disconnect the lug from the fire/overheat transponder.
26-11-01
SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-002
(7) Cut a sufficient length of TFE-R 5/8-X tubing, G50541, or TFE-R-1/2-X tubing, G50544, to
cover the worn area.
(a) Make sure that the sleeve long enough to exceed the length of the damage by
approximately 0.4 in. (10.2 mm).
SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-003
(8) Heat the sleeve at 700°F (371°C) maximum until shrinking operation is completed.
SUBTASK 26-11-01-430-001
(9) Reconnect the lug on the fire detection transponder.
(a) For the fire detector harness, MW0316, make sure that the minimum clearance between
the fire detection wires and strut insulation blanket is 0.15 in. (3.81 mm).
1) If the clearance between the fire detection wires and strut insulation is not within the
limits, do the following steps:
a) Adjust the wires.
b) Secure per SWPM 20-10-11.

A. General
(1) The removal task for harness MW0315 and MW0316 is the same.
(2) MW0315 is a component of loop A of the engine fan fire detection circuit.
(3) MW0316 is a component of loop B of the engine fan fire detection circuit.

E. Fan Fire Detection Harness Removal
SUBTASK 26-11-02-020-001
(1) Fan fire detection harness removal:
(a) Disconnect the harness connector (core) [118] from the core fire detection harness
receptacle.
(b) Disconnect the harness connector (pylon) [119] from the pylon receptacle.
(c) Remove the nut [114] and washer [115] which attach the two terminal lugs of each
harness to the fan fire detectors.
(d) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0315 [101], remove the bolt [112] and washer [113]
that attach each ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket.
(e) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], do these steps:
1) Remove the nut [121] and washer [122] from the one end of the fan fire detection
harness, MW0316 [102] (View F , Figure 401).
2) Remove the bolt [123] and washer [124] from the another end of the fan fire
detection harness, MW0316 [102] (View H, Figure 401).
(f) Loosen the bolt [120] and open the clamp [116] that attach each harness to the engine
brackets.
(g) Disengage the harness from the omega clamps.
(h) Pull the flexible clamp with harness out of the 3:00 o'clock strut and disengage the
harness.
(i) Pull the harness through the 3:00 o'clock strut.
(j) Remove the harness from the engine.

G. Fan Fire Detection Harness Installation
SUBTASK 26-11-02-420-001
(1) Install the fan fire detection harness:
(a) Put the harness in position on the fan frame.
(b) Install the harness through the 3:00 o'clock strut to the inside of the engine.
(c) Distribute the slack all along the attachment points on the fan frame and in the 3:00
o'clock strut.
(d) Connect the harness connector to the core fire detection harness receptacle.
1) Tighten by hand.
(e) Connect the harness connector to the pylon receptacle.
1) Tighten by hand.
(f) For the fire detector harness, MW0315, do these steps:
1) Separate the wires as much as possible.
2) Bend the wires down and away from contact with the strut blanket.
(g) For the fire detector harness, MW0316, do these steps:
1) Achieve the minimum clearance of 0.15 in. (3.81 mm) between the fire detection
wires and strut insulation blanket, do the following steps:
a) Adjust the wires.
b) Secure the wires (SWPM 20-10-11).
2) Make sure that the minimum clearance between the fire detection wires and strut
insulation blanket is 0.15 in. (3.81 mm).
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU HAVE THE HARNESS IN THE CORRECT
POSITION IN THE FIREWALL OPEN HOLES. IF YOU DO NOT INSTALL
HARNESS CORRECTLY , DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(h) Attach the terminal lugs of the harness to each fan fire detector with the washer [115] that
should be installed under the nut [114].
NOTE: If the leads are different sizes, install the lead with the small hole on top of the
lead with the large hole.
1) Tighten the nut [114] to 25.00 in-lb (2.82 N·m) - 35.00 in-lb (3.95 N·m).
(i) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0315 [101], attach the ground lug of the harness to
each engine bracket with the washer [113] and bolt [112].
1) Tighten the bolts [112] to 97 in-lb (11 N·m) - 103 in-lb (11.6 N·m).
(j) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], do these steps:
1) For the one end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], attach the ground
lug of the harness to engine bracket with the washer [122] and nut [121] as follows
(View F , Figure 401):
26-11-02
a) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the contact
surface of the engine bracket and ground lug.
b) Attach the ground lug of the harness to engine bracket with the washer [122]
and nut [121].
c) Tighten the nut [121] to 90 in-lb (10 N·m) - 105 in-lb (11.9 N·m).
d) Measure the electrical resistance between the ground lug and airplane
structure (SWPM 20-20-00).
<1> Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614.
<2> Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0
milliohm) or less.
2) For the another end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], attach the
ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket with the bolt [123] and washer [124]
as follows (View H, Figure 401):
a) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the contact
surface of the engine bracket, ground lug, and washer [124].
b) Attach the ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket with the bolt [123]
and washer [124].
c) Tighten the bolt [123] to 78 in-lb (9 N·m) - 82 in-lb (9.3 N·m).
d) Measure the electrical resistance between the ground lug and airplane
structure (SWPM 20-20-00).
<1> Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614.
<2> Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0
milliohm) or less.
(k) Attach the harness to each engine bracket with the clamp [116] and tighten the bolt [120].
(l) Install the harness in the flexible clamp at the 3:00 o'clock strut location.
(m) Engage the harness in the omega clamps.
SUBTASK 26-11-02-410-001
(2) Close the access to the 3:00 o'clock position strut as follows:
(a) Install the 3:00 O'clock strut seal [105], washers [107], and bolts [106].
1) Tighten the bolts [106] to 110.0 in-lb (12.4 N·m) - 120.0 in-lb (13.6 N·m).
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE HARNESSES ARE IN THE CORRECT POSITION
IN THE FIREWALLS. IF THE HARNESSES ARE NOT IN THE CORRECT
POSITION, IT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
(b) Install the lower half-firewall [109] and attach it to the strut with the bolts [108].
1) Use graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101], to lubricate the threads of the
bolts [108].
2) Tighten the bolts [108] to 109.25 in-lb (12.34 N·m) - 120.75 in-lb (13.64 N·m).
(c) Install the metal plug [125].
(d) Install the upper half-firewall [103] and attach it to the lower half fire wall with the
bolts [104].
1) Use graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101], to lubricate the threads of the
bolts [104].
26-11-02
2) Tighten the bolts [104] to 109.25 in-lb (12.34 N·m) - 120.75 in-lb (13.64 N·m).
(e) Install the 3:00 O'clock strut fairing [111] and attach it with the screws [110] to the 3:00
o'clock strut.
1) Tighten the screws [110] to 30 in-lb (3.4 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4.0 N·m).

G. Core Fire Detection Harness Installation
SUBTASK 26-11-02-420-002
(1) The core fire detection harness, MW0326 [31] and core fire detection harness, MW0325 [33]
installation.
(a) Put the core fire detection harness, MW0326 [31] and core fire detection harness,
MW0325 [33] in position on the engine.
(b) Attach the harness receptacle, DJ2601, and harness receptacle, DJ2501, to the engine
bracket with the bolts [35] on each receptacle.
1) Tighten the bolts [35] to 73 in-lb (8.2 N·m) - 77 in-lb (8.7 N·m).
(c) Connect the fan fire detection harness connector, DP1601, and harness connector,
DP1501, to the harness receptacle, DJ2601, and harness receptacle DJ2501.
1) Tighten by hand.
CAUTION
DO NOT BEND OR APPLY SIDE LOADS TO THE WIRE LUGS. DAMAGED
WIRE LUGS CAUSE LOOP DETECTOR FAULTS. IF YOU BEND THE
WIRE LUGS, DAMAGE TO THEM OCCURS.
(d) Attach the terminal lugs [20] to each core fire detector with the washer [15] and nut [14].
1) If the leads are different sizes, install the lead with the small hole on top of the lead
with the large hole.
2) Tighten the nuts [14] to 25 in-lb (2.82 N·m) - 35 in-lb (3.95 N·m).
RYR ALL PRE SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0258 AND PRE SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-1010 AND PRE SB
737-CFM56-7B-72-0981 AND PRE SB 737-26-1123 AND PRE SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0759

(1) Remove only enough length of the wire harness to provide sufficient access to the lug.

bracket to disconnect a ground lug.
(b) Remove the nut [3] and washer [4] which secure the terminal lugs to the fire detector to
disconnect a terminal wire.
(c) Remove the lug insulator.
(d) Cut the cable at the lug barrel.
1) Make sure that the cut at the wire and insulation of the cable is clean and clear.
(e) Discard the damaged lug.

E. Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning
SUBTASK 26-14-01-860-001
(1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
E 12 C01125 LAVATORY SMOKE
26-14-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS (Continued)
SUBTASK 26-14-01-160-001
WARNING
WHEN YOU CLEAN THE SMOKE DETECTOR AND SENSOR, MAKE SURE
THAT THE AREA HAS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. KEEP THE ALCOHOL AWAY
FROM HEAT , SPARKS OR FLAMES. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM
THE ALCOHOL. PUT ON RUBBER GLOVES. ALCOHOL IS A FLAMMABLE
AND POISONOUS SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Put on the rubber glove, STD-1086.
SUBTASK 26-14-01-160-002
(3) Remove the dirt from the exterior of the case cover [1] with the cotton wiper, G00034, soaked
with alcohol, B00065.
SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-001
(4) Do these steps to remove and disassemble the smoke sensor assembly [4]:
(a) Insert the pocket scale between the smoke sensor assembly [4] and case cover [1].
(b) Push the spring lever [2] with the pocket scale and pull out the smoke sensor
assembly [4].
(c) Slide the screwdriver between each side of the sensor cell assembly [6] and cover [7]
and twist the screwdriver to release the lockpins.
(d) Remove the cover [7] from the sensor cell assembly [6].
WARNING
DO NOT DISASSEMBLE THE SENSOR CELL ASSEMBLY . THE SENSOR
CELL CONTAINS RADIOACTIVE MATERIAL. THE RADIOACTIVE
MATERIAL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL.
CAUTION
DO NOT TOUCH THE ELECTRODE IN THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY .
DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR CAN OCCUR.
(e) Pull the mesh [8] from the sensor cell assembly [6].
SUBTASK 26-14-01-210-001
(5) Do these steps to clean the mesh [8] and sensor cell assembly [6]:
(a) Examine the mesh [8] for contamination.
(b) To remove contamination, clean the mesh [8] with general purpose household detergent
cleaner, B00541, and warm water.
(c) If the mesh [8] is very dirty, replace it with a new mesh [8].
(d) With the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, dry the mesh [8] completely.
CAUTION
BLOW THE AIR LIGHTLY INTO THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY . TOO MUCH
FORCE CAN CAUSE STATIC ELECTRICITY ON THE ELECTRODES
WHICH CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR.
(e) With the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, lightly blow any dirt or particles out of the
sensor cell assembly [6].
26-14-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS (Continued)
SUBTASK 26-14-01-420-001
(6) Do these steps to assemble and install the smoke sensor assembly [4]:
(a) Install the mesh [8] on the sensor cell assembly [6].
(b) Align the hole in the cover [7] with the hole in the sensor cell assembly [6].
(c) Install the cover [7] on the sensor cell assembly [6].
(d) Install the smoke sensor assembly [4] in the case cover [1].
SUBTASK 26-14-01-860-002
(7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
E 12 C01125 LAVATORY SMOKE
SUBTASK 26-14-01-710-001
(8) Do this task: Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test, TASK 26-14-00-730-801.

SUBTASK 26-14-01-000-004
CAUTION
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE OR INSTALL THE SCREEN OF THE
COVER ASSEMBLY AND LABYRINTH. THIS WILL HELP PREVENT
SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH. SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH CAN

DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES.
(2) Disassemble the lavatory smoke detector as follows:
(a) Remove the screws [13] and washers [14] that attach the cover/Electro-Magnetic
Interference (EMI) gasket assembly to the housing/screen assembly [11].
26-14-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued)
(b) Remove the slide-lock-post [16] and washer [17] that attach the bonding jumper [19] to
the connector [18].
NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one washer, one hex nut, and one
slide-lock-post.
(c) Remove the remaining slide-lock-post [16] and washer [17] that attach the connector [18]
to the housing/screen assembly [11].
NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one hex nut, one lock washer, one flat washer,
and one slide-lock-post.
(d) Pull the connector [18] back inside the housing/screen assembly [11].
(e) Remove the gasket [20].
(f) Remove the EMI gasket [28] from the cover [15].
1) If it is necessary, remove the nut [31], lock washer [30], and flat washer [29] to
disconnect the bonding jumper [32] from the cover/EMI gasket assembly.
(g) Remove the screws [21] and washers [22] that connect the CCA and labyrinth
assembly [12] to the housing/screen assembly [11].
(h) Remove the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12].
(i) Disconnect the connector [27] from the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12].
(j) Remove the fasteners [23], washers [24], and nuts [25] that attach the labyrinth top [26]
to the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12].
F . Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning
SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-003
CAUTION
ESDS COMPONENTS AND UNITS MUST BE PROTECTED FROM STATIC
DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES.
(1) Clean the labyrinth assembly.
(a) Make a solution of soap and water with one part soap, B50026, and twenty parts water,
G50527.
WARNING
WHEN YOU USE THE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN, TO COOL OR TO
DRY , DO NOT EXCEED 30 PSIG (207 KPA). MAKE SURE YOU WEAR
EYE PROTECTION AND DO NOT DIRECT COMPRESSED AIR AT
YOURSELF AND OTHERS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
CAUTION
DO NOT PUT THE LABYRINTH FULLY IN THE SOAP AND WATER
SOLUTION WHEN YOU CLEAN THE LABYRINTH. LARGE QUANTITIES
OF THE SOAP AND WATER SOLUTION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
LABYRINTH.
(b) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, and dry, clean air source 0 to 60 psi, STD-4107, at
maximum pressure of 30 psig (207 kPa) to remove the unwanted material from the inner
area of the labyrinth.
NOTE: You can also use the soap and water solution. Remove the soap and water
solution with clean water.
26-14-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued)
(c) Clean the inner area of the labyrinth with a clean cotton swab, STD-6402, moist with the
soap and water solution.
(d) Dry the lenses with a clean and dry cotton swab, STD-6402.
(e) Remove the remaining soap or contamination from the lenses.
(f) Make sure that the labyrinth assembly is fully dry before you assemble the smoke
detector.
SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-004
(2) If it is necessary, clean the screen of the housing/screen assembly [11].
WARNING
WHEN YOU USE THE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN, TO COOL OR TO
DRY , DO NOT EXCEED 30 PSIG (207 KPA). MAKE SURE YOU WEAR
EYE PROTECTION AND DO NOT DIRECT COMPRESSED AIR AT
YOURSELF AND OTHERS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(a) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, or dry, clean air source 0 to 60 psi, STD-4107, at
maximum pressure of 30 psig (207 kPa) to remove unwanted materials from the screen.
SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-005
(3) Do these steps to clean all non-electrical parts:
WARNING
ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS FLAMMABLE. AVOID PROLONGED
BREATHING OF VAPOR AND PROLONGED OR REPEATED CONTACT

NOTE: This tryout is to make sure that the APU fire detection system is in a zero energy state.
SUBTASK 26-15-00-210-001

not come on.

1) The APU remote fire light, on the P28 panel, does not come on.
2) The APU fire warning horn does not come on.

26-15-00-710-801APU Fire Detection - Operational TestPDF p. 323
Open original AMM PDF - page 323 ↗ 
2. APU Fire Detection - Operational Test
(Figure 501)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) The operational test does a check of the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire detection system
without disturbing the detection system and it only uses the equipment that is on the airplane.
This test will verify the circuit from the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, to
the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, and to the P28 APU remote panel. The test will
make sure that the engine and APU fire detection control module, that continuously monitors
the detection loops, operates correctly.
NOTE: If the APU fire detection system is inoperative, the FIRE WARN lights and ALARM
BELL are not activated. Only if the engines and APU are functioning properly, the FIRE
WARN lights and ALARM BELL will be activated. The circuit design precludes latent
failure modes due to the parallel path of the test circuit and actuation circuit.
NOTE: If the Alternating Current (AC) power is removed (no power on main buses) and if
there is a faulty APU LOOP , the FIRE BELL, FIRE WARNING light, MASTER
CAUTION, and OVHT/DET lights are not activated.
B. References
Reference Title
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right

SUBTASK 26-15-00-860-001
(1) Make sure that the electrical power is supplied (TASK 24-22-00-860-811).
SUBTASK 26-15-00-860-002

E. FIRE/OVHT Test
SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-001
(1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the
OVHT/FIRE position.
NOTE: When performing the Fire/OVHT test with a faulted APU fire detector loop, the
MASTER CAUTION lights are off, the FIRE WARN and OVHT/DET lights are off, the
fire bell does not sound and the APU DET INOP light will be on.
NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the engine and wheel well detection system and the
indications, on the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the
electronic equipment bay.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the APU Fire Switch light
(red) comes on.
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red).
3) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
(c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, in the aural warning module, M315,
comes on.
(d) In the right main wheel well, make sure that these conditions occur:
1) The APU remote fire light (red), on the P28 APU remote panel, flashes on and off
continuously.
2) The APU fire warning horn comes on.
SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-002
(2) Release the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the APU Fire Switch light
(red) goes off.
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights goes off:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red).
3) The OVHT/DET light (amber).
(c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, in the aural warning module, M315, stops.
(d) In the right main wheel well, make sure that these conditions occur:
1) The APU remote fire light (red), on the P28 APU remote panel, goes off.
26-15-00
2) The APU fire warning horn stops.
F . FAULT/INOP Test from the P8-1 Engine and APU Fire Control Panel
SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-003
(1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the
FAULT/INOP position.
NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the engine detection system and the indications, on the
engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the electronic equipment bay.
(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) APU DET INOP light (amber).
NOTE: When performing the FAULT/INOP Test with a faulted APU detector loop,
the APU DET INOP light will stay on, the MASTER CAUTION lights are off
and the FAULT light will come on during the test.
2) FAULT light (amber)
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).

(a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights are off:

2) FAULT light (amber).
(b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights are off:
1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber).
2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).

2. APU Overheat Detector Element Removal
(Figure 401)

F . APU Overheat Detector Element Removal
SUBTASK 26-15-01-000-001
(1) Do these steps to remove the detector element [7] from the detector housing.
(a) Loosen the screw [10] on each clamp [9].
(b) Remove the nut [5] and washer [4] from the terminal lug.
(c) Disconnect the lead from the terminal lug.
(d) Remove the four screws [2], washers [16], and nuts [17] from the overheat detector
housing.
(e) Remove the element [7] from the clamp [9].
(f) Remove the grommets [8] from the element [7] to use on the new element.
(g) Remove the element [7].

H. APU Overheat Detector Element Installation
SUBTASK 26-15-01-420-001
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU STAY AWAY FROM ELEVATOR PARTS THAT MOVE.
INTERNAL ELEMENTS FROM THE ELEVATOR SYSTEM CAN MOVE
SUDDENLY IF THE AIRCRAFT IS OPEN TO WIND. DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do these steps to install an overheat detector element [7]:
(a) Make sure that the detector housing and mounting bracket are clean.
(b) Put the overheat detector element [7] in position along the support tube [6].
(c) Put the overheat detector housing in the correct position on the mounting bracket.
(d) Install the screws [2], washers [16], and nuts [17] that attach the overheat detector
housing to the mounting bracket.
1) Tighten the nuts [17] to 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m) to 30 in-lb (3.4 N·m).
(e) Install the grommets [8] on the detector element [7] at each clamp [9] location.
(f) Install the element [7] in the clamp [9].
(g) Make sure that the end of the detector element [7] ends less than 1 in. (25 mm) past the
last grommet [8].
1) If the detector element [7] extends more than 1 in. (25 mm), adjust the length of the
element between each clamp [9] so that any slack is evenly distributed.
(h) Make sure that there is at least 0.12 in. (3.0 mm) between the overheat detector
element [7] and support tube [6].
26-15-01
CAUTION
FOR THE LAST ASSEMBLY , USE LUBRICATED SCREWS. IF SCREWS
ARE REUSED, OR IF NON-LUBRICATED SCREWS ARE USED THEY
CAN FREEZE BECAUSE OF HIGH TEMPERATURES. THE THREADS ON
THESE SCREWS MUST HAVE A LAYER OF COMPOUND, D00004,
BEFORE INSTALLATION.
(i) Apply compound, D00004, to the screw [10] threads.
(j) Tighten the screw [10] on each clamp [9] until 1-1/2 to 3 threads extend through the nut.
1) Make sure that the grommets [8] and clamp [9] are tight.
2) Make sure that the ends of the screws [10] do not touch the support tube [6].
(k) Apply compound, D00004, to the threads of the terminal lug.
(l) Put the lead in position on the terminal lug, and attach with a washer [4] and nut [5].
(m) Tighten the nut [5] to 32.0 in-lb (3.6 N·m) to 38.0 in-lb (4.3 N·m).
(n) Apply Silcoset 152 adhesive, A50154, to the terminal lugs.
SUBTASK 26-15-01-280-001
(2) Do a check of the resistance between the element stud and the airplane structure.
(a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550, or equivalent, that can
measure 0.0001 ohm (0.1 milliohm).
(b) Make sure that the resistance is less than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms).
(c) If the resistance is more than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms), clean the bonding surfaces
with the solvent, B00083, and do the check again.

H. APU Overheat Detector Assembly Installation
SUBTASK 26-15-01-420-002
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU STAY AWAY FROM ELEVATOR PARTS THAT MOVE.
INTERNAL ELEMENTS FROM THE ELEVATOR SYSTEM CAN MOVE
SUDDENLY IF THE AIRCRAFT IS OPEN TO WIND. DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do these steps to install a overheat detector assembly [1]:
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH UPPER AND LOWER OVERHEAT DETECTORS
(a) Put the detector assembly in position in the APU compartment, and install the clamps
[13].
1) Attach each clamp [13] with two bolts [11] and washers [12].
2) Tighten the bolts [11] to 32.0 in-lb (3.6 N·m) to 38.0 in-lb (4.3 N·m).
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH TAILPIPE OVERHEAT DETECTOR
(b) Put the detector assembly in position in the APU compartment.
1) Put the clamp [14] and clamp [15] in position and attach with the bolt [11] and
washer [12].
2) Tighten the bolts to 32.0 in-lb (3.6 N·m) to 38.0 in-lb (4.3 N·m).
RYR ALL
(c) Install the two bolts [3] that attach the overheat detector housing to the APU
compartment.
(d) Tighten the bolts to 72.0 in-lb (8 N·m) to 88 in-lb (10 N·m).
(e) Put the lead in position on the terminal lug, and attach with a washer [4] and nut [5].
(f) Tighten the nut [5] to 32.0 in-lb (3.6 N·m) to 38.0 in-lb (4.3 N·m).
(g) Apply Silcoset 152 adhesive, A50154 sealant to the terminal lugs.
SUBTASK 26-15-01-280-002
(2) Do a check of the resistance between the stud and the airplane structure.
(a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550, or equivalent, which can
measure 0.0001 ohm (0.1 milliohm).
(b) Make sure the resistance is less than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms).
(c) If the resistance is more than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms), clean the bonding surfaces
with the solvent, B00083, and do the check again.

D. APU Fire Warning Horn Removal
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-004
(1) Remove screws [2] and washers [3] that connect the APU fire warning horn [1] to the P28
panel bracket.
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-008
(2) Carefully move the APU fire warning horn [1] away from the P28 panel bracket to get sufficient
access to disconnect the electrical wire terminals [4].
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-001
(3) Tag each electrical wire with its location.
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-002
(4) Loosen the electrical wire terminals [4].
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-003
(5) Disconnect the electrical wire terminals [4] from the APU fire warning horn [1].
SUBTASK 26-15-02-020-005
(6) Remove the APU fire warning horn [1].

(3) Apply fay seal with sealant, A50110, or sealant, A50155, or sealant, A50231, or sealant,

26-16-00-040-801Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - DeactivationPDF p. 363
Open original AMM PDF - page 363 ↗ 
2. Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Deactivation
A. General
(1) This task will deactivate the cargo bay smoke detection system.
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 26-16-00-020-001
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
C 16 C01523 CARGO FIRE FWD DET B
C 17 C01522 CARGO FIRE FWD DET A
C 18 C01525 CARGO FIRE AFT DET B
C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A
C. Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Tryout
NOTE: This tryout is to make sure that the cargo bay smoke detection system is in a zero energy
state.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-210-001
(1) Push and hold the TEST switch on the Cargo Fire Control Panel.
(a) On the Cargo Fire Control Panel, make sure that none of these lights come on:
1) The FWD CARGO SMOKE light.
2) The AFT CARGO SMOKE light.
3) The DETECTOR FAULT light.
(b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure that none of these lights come on:
1) The Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights.
(c) Make sure the flight compartment fire bell does not come on.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-210-002
(2) Release the TEST switch.

B. Cargo Bay Smoke Detection Operational Test
SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-017
(1) Make sure FWD and AFT DET SELECT switches on the Cargo Fire Control Panel are set to
NORM.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-710-001
(2) Push and hold the TEST switch on the Cargo Fire Control Panel.
(a) On the Cargo Fire Control panel, look for these indications:
1) The ARM FWD and AFT red lights comes on.
2) The DISCH light comes on.
3) The EXT FWD and EXT AFT light comes on.
4) The DETECTOR FAULT light is off.
(b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure these lights come on:
1) The Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights (Red).
(c) Make sure you hear the flight compartment fire bell.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-710-003
(3) Push the Captain's or First Officer's FIRE WARN switch.
NOTE: If you push the either of the FIRE WARN lights on the P7 panel, or the BELL CUTOUT
switch on the Engine and APU Fire Control panel, the same conditions will occur. The
FIRE WARN and BELL CUTOUT switches cause the same resets but from different
locations.
(a) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure these lights go off:
1) The Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights (Red).
26-16-00

26-16-00-730-801Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Smoke TestPDF p. 373
Open original AMM PDF - page 373 ↗ 
3. Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Smoke Test
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) When the DET SELECT switch is set to A, and any detector in loop A detects smoke, an alarm
will occur. Similarly, when the DET SELECT switch is set to B, and the B loop detects smoke,
the alarm will occur. When the DET SELECT switch is set to NORM, smoke must be detected
by a detector in loop A and loop B (any detector in both loops, they do not have to be a pair)
before the alarm will occur.
NOTE: If you apply too much smoke to a detector, the excess smoke could affect adjacent
detectors. Try to limit the amount of smoke applied to each detector.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.
Reference Description
COM-11202 Smoke Generator - Lavatory/Crew Rest Smoke Detector (or 737
Cargo Area Type Smoke Detectors)
Part #: HO-25S Supplier: 61908
Part #: SOLOA10 Supplier: 1WU32
Opt Part #: SOLOA3 Supplier: 1WU32
Opt Part #: SOLOA4 Supplier: 1WU32
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left
122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right
141 Aft Cargo Compartment - Left
142 Aft Cargo Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right

(1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:

C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A
E. Smoke Test
SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-006
(1) On the Cargo Fire Control panel P8, set the DET SELECT FWD switch to A.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-007
(2) On the Cargo Fire Control pane P8, set the DET SELECT AFT switch to NORM.
26-16-00

(3) Use a smoke generator, COM-11202, to make smoke adjacent to the forward left detector in
the forward cargo bay.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-006
(4) Make sure that these indications occur:
(a) On the Cargo Bay Fire Control panel, the ARM FWD light (red) comes on.
(b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights (red)
come on.
(c) In the flight compartment, the fire bell comes on.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-003
(5) On the Cargo Fire Control panel, set the DET SELECT FWD and DET SELECT AFT switch to
NORM.
(a) Make sure all the alarm indications stop.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-008
(6) Clear the smoke from the cargo bay.
NOTE: If the smoke is not cleared from the cargo bay within 60 seconds, the smoke
indications will occur again.
SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-004

SUBTASK 26-16-01-860-001
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:

26-16-01
SUBTASK 26-16-01-160-001
WARNING
WHEN YOU CLEAN THE SMOKE DETECTOR AND SENSOR, MAKE SURE
THAT THE AREA HAS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. KEEP THE ALCOHOL AWAY
FROM HEAT , SPARKS OR FLAMES. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM
THE ALCOHOL. PUT ON RUBBER GLOVES. ALCOHOL IS A FLAMMABLE
AND POISONOUS SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS AN AGENT THAT IS FLAMMABLE, EXPLOSIVE,
AND POISONOUS. MAKE SURE ALL PERSONS OBEY ALL OF THE
PRECAUTIONS WHEN ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS USED.
• GAS/AIR MIXTURES MORE THAN THE LOWER EXPLOSIVE LIMIT (LEL)
CAN CAUSE AN EXPLOSION IF HIGH HEAT , SPARKS, OR FLAMES
SUPPLY IGNITION.
• USE IN AN AREA OPEN TO THE AIR.
• CLOSE THE CONTAINER WHEN NOT USED.
• DO NOT GET ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IN THE EYES, ON THE SKIN, OR ON
YOUR CLOTHES.
• DO NOT BREATHE THE GAS.
(2) Put on rubber glove, STD-1086.
SUBTASK 26-16-01-160-002
(3) Remove the dirt from the exterior of the smoke detector with the cotton wiper, G00034, soaked
with alcohol, B00065.
SUBTASK 26-16-01-210-001
CAUTION
BLOW THE AIR LIGHTLY INTO THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY . TOO MUCH
FORCE CAN CAUSE STATIC ELECTRICITY ON THE ELECTRODES WHICH
CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR.
(4) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, and compressed nitrogen at a maximum pressure of 15
psig, or with the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, lightly blow any dirt or particles out of the
sensor assembly.
NOTE: Scheduled cleaning is not necessary, but the smoke detector labyrinth must be kept
clean to operate accurately.
(a) Clean the connector.
(b) Clean the dust screens.
(c) Clean the inner area of the labyrinth.
NOTE: Refer to the applicable CMM for the instructions. It is not necessary to fully
disassemble the smoke detector to clean the inner area of the labyrinth.
(5) Clean the electrical connector pins with alcohol, B50073.
(6) Install the cover if removed.

D. Overheat Detection System Operational Test - Flight Compartment
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-001
(1) Move and hold the TEST switch on the Engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) Fire Control
Panel to the OVHT/FIRE position.
(a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel comes on.
(b) Make sure the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights on the Pilot's Glareshield,
P7, come on.
(c) Make sure the flight compartment fire bell in the Aural Warning Module, M315, comes on.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-013
(2) Push the BELL CUTOUT switch on the P8-1 panel.
(a) Make sure the fire bell goes off immediately.
(b) Make sure that both FIRE WARN lights go off.
(c) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel remains on.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-014
(3) Set the LIGHTS switch on the pilots main panel, P1 to DIM.
(a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light dims.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-015
(4) Set the LIGHTS switch on the pilots main panel, P1 to BRT .
(a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light returns to its original brightness.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-002
(5) Release the TEST switch on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel.
(a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel goes off.
(b) Make sure the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights on the Pilot's Glareshield
remains off.
(c) Make sure the flight compartment fire bell in the Aural Warning Module, M315, remains
off.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-003
(6) Push and hold the OVHT TEST switch on the air conditioning panel, P5-10, for at least 5
seconds.
AIRCRAFT MAINTENANCE MANUAL 26-18-00

1) The left WING-BODY OVERHEAT light.
2) The right WING-BODY OVERHEAT light.

1) The AIR COND light.
2) The Captain's and First Officer's MASTER CAUTION lights.
SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-004

(b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure these lights go off:
1) The AIR COND light.
2) The Captain's and First Officer's MASTER CAUTION lights.
