2. Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit, M237
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The wheel well fire, wing and body overheat detection control module, M237 is located on the
E1-4 shelf in the Electronic Equipment Compartment (EEC). The wheel well fire, wing & body
overheat detection control module will be referred to as the control module throughout this
procedure. Access the front of panel of the control module to do the Built-In-Test Equipment
(BITE) test.
(2) This task gives instructions to perform these procedures with the control module:
(a) Display Test Procedure
(b) Local Test Procedure
(c) Memory Read Procedure
(d) Memory Clear Procedure
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
(e) Zone Resistance Test.
RYR ALL
(3) Do not hold the control module switches in the CLOSED position for more than 10 seconds or
the software could stop the test.
(4) Unless the test specifies differently, continue from one step to the subsequent step in less than
40 seconds.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
C. BITE Procedure
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-001
(1) Do these steps to do the display procedure:
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300
(a) Push and release the DISP TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module.
1) Make sure the code 88 comes on, then goes off.
26-18-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
(b) Push and release the DISPLAY TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection
module.
1) Make sure all Light Emitting Diode (LED)s come on, then go off.
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-002
(2) Do these steps to do the local test procedure for the control module:
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300
(a) Push and release the LOC TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module.
1) Make sure the FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code 90 comes on while the
test is in progress, then the code 99 comes on to show the test is complete.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
(b) Push and release the LOCAL TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection
module.
1) Make sure the FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code CONFIG NEXTGEN1
comes on while the self-test is in progress, then the code TEST COMPLETE comes
on to show there are no faults.
NOTE: If other codes are displayed, faults exist in the system which must be
corrected.
RYR ALL
(c) If an alarm/fault condition is found, the display will show a fault code.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300
1) Refer to the table at the end of this task to find the fault isolation task for the
applicable maintenance message for the fault indicated by the FAULT & ALARM
CODE display.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
2) Refer to the table at the end of this task to find the fault isolation task for the
applicable maintenance message for the fault indicated by the FAULT & ALARM
CODES display.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-003
(3) Do these steps to do the memory read procedure:
(a) Push the MEM READ switch on the compartment overheat detection module.
1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows the latest fault code.
NOTE: As many as 10 alarm/fault codes can be kept in non-volatile memory. Code
97 shows that all codes have been read.
(b) Push the MEM READ switch again until all the alarm/fault codes have been read, and
code 97 shows.
(c) Push the MEM READ switch again so the display shows no data.
26-18-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-004
(4) Do these steps to do the memory read procedure:
(a) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch on the compartment overheat detection
module.
1) Make sure the code END OF MEMORY comes on to show the test is complete.
2) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again until all the alarm/faults have
been read.
NOTE: A minimum of 10 Line Replaceable Unit (LRU) failures can be stored in
non-volatile memory.
3) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again so the display shows no data.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-005
(5) Do these steps to do the memory clear procedure.
(a) Push the DISP TEST switch on the control module.
1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code 88 and then the display is blank.
(b) Push the LOC TEST switch on the control module.
1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows 90, then 99.
NOTE: After code 99 is shown, continue with the next step in less than 10 seconds.
2) If code 93 or 94 is displayed, push and release LOC TEST until code 99 is shown.
NOTE: After code 99 is shown, continue with the next step in less than 10 seconds.
(c) Push and release the MEM READ switch on the control module until the display shows
the code to be removed.
NOTE: If there are fault codes in the memory, the display will show the last code that
was recorded. If there are no fault codes in the memory, the display will show
code 97.
(d) Push and release the MEM CLEAR switch to clear the displayed code.
1) The display shows no data.
(e) Push and release the MEM READ switch again to display the next code.
1) Repeat the two steps above until code 97 appears.
NOTE: Alarm/fault codes can not be removed until the condition is corrected.
(f) Push the MEM READ switch again so the display shows no data and the MAINT ADV
light is off.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-006
(6) Do these steps to do the memory clear procedure.
(a) Push and hold the MEMORY CLEAR switch on the control module.
(b) When MEMORY CLEAR displays, release the switch.
NOTE: This can be approximately 5 seconds.
26-18-01
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (Continued)
(c) Make sure that the MAINT ADV light stays off.
NOTE: If there are fault codes in the memory, the display will show the last code that
was recorded. If there are no fault codes in the memory, the display will show no
data.
(d) Push and release the MEMORY CLEAR switch to clear the displayed code.
1) The display shows no data.
(e) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again to display the next code.
1) Repeat the two steps above until no data shows.
NOTE: Alarm/fault codes can not be removed until the condition is corrected.
(f) Push the MEMORY READ switch again so the display shows no data and the MAINT
ADV light is off.
SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-007

(CODM) BITE):

(b) Continue to push and release LOCAL TEST . This will cycle through the different zones
followed by LOOP ___ which shows the resistance value in the loop or 2GND_____
which shows the resistance value to ground.
NOTE: If the loops are functioning properly all zones will have a 2GND value of 5000.
(c) Make sure that the resistance value agree with the Table 501 below:

26-18-01

(d) Select Loop: [All].

(f) Select the applicable loops that are to be scanned.
(g) it's Select Launch Scan.
1) If the scan takes longer than two minutes, restart the hardware.
(h) When scan is finished, select PDF Report.
(i) Review the PDF report to identify possible problems among the reported items.
1) If problems are found, the report will show the location of the problem.
2) Correct the problems and perform the automated test again.
3) When the problems have been corrected, there will be no problems listed in the
report.

G. Long Sensor Element - Removal
NOTE: This procedure only applies to the following sensors:
element [15], M356, R FWD A/C PACK
element [16], M370, L WING INBOARD
element [16], M371, R WING INBOARD
element [17], M268, L WING OUTBOARD

element [25], M272, FWD KEELBEAM
element [28], M355, L FWD A/C PACK
element [29], M270, WHEEL WELL.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-001
(1) Loosen all the bolts [6] securing the mounting clips [4] that hold the sensor element in its
position.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-002
(2) Remove the sensor element from all the mounting clips [4].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-015
(3) Remove the grommet [5] from the sensor element.
NOTE: Keep the grommet to reuse during installation procedure.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-003
(4) For each sensor element in the wing and lower aft body, do these steps to disconnect the
connector [3] at one end of the sensor element:
(a) Remove the lockwires [1] from the jamnut [2].
(b) If it is necessary, disconnect the coupling element connector or electrical connector and
remove the jamnut [2].
(c) Disconnect the connector [3].
(d) Put covers on the open end of the connectors [3] with caps to prevent damage.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-016
(5) For each sensor element in the main wheel well, do these steps to disconnect the
connector [3] at one end of the sensor element:
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-094 PRE SB 737-26-1124;
AIRPLANES WITHOUT MAIN WHEEL WELL CORROSION PROTECTION
(a) Remove the lockwire [1] (MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347) from the jamnut [2].
(b) Loosen the jamnut [2].
26-18-02
RYR 095-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220,
223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 006, 007, 013,
014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-094 POST SB 737-26-1124; AIRPLANES WITH
MAIN WHEEL WELL CORROSION PROTECTION
(c) Remove the nut [8], two washers [9], and bolt [11] from the terminal lug [10].
(d) Remove the lockwire [1] (MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347) from the connector hex
nut [2].
(e) Loosen the connector hex nut [2].
RYR ALL
(f) Put covers on the open end of the connector [3] with caps to prevent damage.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-004
(6) Hold the sensor element carefully to keep it in its position and to prevent bends.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-005
(7) For each sensor element in the wing and lower aft body, do these steps to disconnect the
connector [3] at the other end of the element:
(a) Remove the lockwires [1] from the jamnut [2].
(b) If it is necessary, disconnect the coupling element connector or electrical connector and
remove the jamnut [2].
(c) Disconnect the connector [3].
(d) Put covers on the open end of the connector [3] with tape or caps to prevent damage.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-017
(8) For each sensor element in the main wheel well, do these steps to disconnect the
connector [3] at the other end of the sensor element:
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-094 PRE SB 737-26-1124;
AIRPLANES WITHOUT MAIN WHEEL WELL CORROSION PROTECTION
(a) Remove the lockwire [1] (MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347) from the jamnut [2].
(b) Loosen the jamnut [2].
RYR 095-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220,
223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 006, 007, 013,

SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-006
(9) For each sensor element on the aft keel beam, do these steps:
(a) Tie a cord to the forward end of the sensor element.
1) Make sure that the cord is longer than the sensor element.
(b) Slowly pull the sensor element aft through the keel beam.
1) Use the access holes below the keel beam to help guide the sensor element
through the holes in the structure.
26-18-02
(c) Remove the sensor element from the cord and leave the cord in its position inside the
keel beam.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-550-001
(10) Carefully roll the sensor element into a 6 in. (15.2 cm) diameter coil.
(a) Use tape if it is necessary.
H. Looped Sensor Element - Removal
NOTE: This procedure only applies to the following sensors:
element [13], M1912, R AFT A/C PACK
element [14], M1910, R MID A/C PACK
element [18], M1763, R FWD STRUT
element [18], M1761, L STRUT FWD
element [30], M1762, L STRUT AFT
element [30], M1764, R AFT STRUT
element [26], M1911, L AFT A/C PACK
element [27], M1909, L MID A/C PACK.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-019
(1) If necessary, remove the bracket when removing the element [18], M1763, R FWD STRUTor
element [18], M1761, L STRUT FWD.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-007
(2) Remove the nut [8], two washers [9], and bolt [11] that secure each lead to the terminal
lug [10].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-020
(3) Remove the jumper from the bracket [12].
(a) For the Air Conditioning (A/C) pack locations, remove the screw [43], washers [32], and
nut [51].
(b) For the forward strut locations, remove the nut [33] and washer [34] from the
jumpers [47].
(c) For the aft strut locations, remove the screw [50], washers [48], and nut [49] from the
jumper [46].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-013
(4) For the strut locations, release the elements from the clamps [35].
(a) Open the clamp [35] to free the element.
(b) Remove the grommet [5] from the element.
NOTE: Keep the grommet for the installation procedure.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-021
(5) Remove the bracket [12] with the element.
(a) For the A/C pack locations, remove the screws [36] and washers [37].
(b) For the forward strut locations, remove the bolts [38] and washers [39].
(c) For the aft strut locations, remove the screws [44] and washers [45].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-022
(6) Place the bracket [12] with the element on a clean work surface.
26-18-02
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-023
(7) Release the element from the clamp [35] on the bracket [12].
(a) Open the clamp [35] to free the element.
(b) Remove the grommet [5] from the element.
NOTE: Keep the grommet for the installation procedure.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-009
(8) Remove the lockwire from the jamnuts [2] (TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-018
(9) Remove the jamnut [2] securing each connector [3] to the bracket [12].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-020-010
(10) Remove the sensor element from the bracket [12].

I. Long Sensor Element - Installation
NOTE: This procedure only applies to the following sensors:
element [15], M356, R FWD A/C PACK
element [16], M370, L WING INBOARD
element [16], M371, R WING INBOARD
element [17], M268, L WING OUTBOARD
element [17], M269, R WING OUTBOARD
element [19], M276, AFT - SECTION 48
element [20], M275, AFT - SECTION 47
element [21], M347, AFT - SECTION 46
element [22], M1147, AFT - SECTION 46
element [23], M348, AFT - SECTION 46
element [24], M273, AFT KEELBEAM
element [25], M272, FWD KEELBEAM
element [28], M355, L FWD A/C PACK
26-18-02
element [29], M270, WHEEL WELL.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-001
CAUTION
WHEN YOU INSTALL THE ELEMENT , DO NOT BEND THE ELEMENT TO A
RADIUS THAT IS LESS THAN 1 IN. (25.4 MM.). DO NOT LET A BEND IN 1 IN.
(25.4 MM.) DISTANCE FROM THE EDGE OF THE QUICK-RELEASE CLAMP
OR THE FLANGE ASSEMBLY . CONTOUR THE ELEMENT TO CLEAR
ADJACENT PARTS AND STRUCTURES. HAVE THE ELEMENT LENGTH
EQUALLY BETWEEN CLAMPS. IF THE BEND RADIUS IS LESS THAN ONE
INCH, DAMAGE TO THE ELEMENT CAN OCCUR.
CAUTION
DO NOT TURN, PULL, OR ATTACH THE SENSING ELEMENT WITH A CLAMP .
DO NOT MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT . IF
YOU MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT , DAMAGE
TO THE SENSING ELEMENT CAN OCCUR.
(1) Carefully put the element into position.
NOTE: This step will require two people.
(a) Carefully unwind the element and make sure that there are no kinks.
(b) Carefully bend the element to fit into its approximate position.
(c) Carefully put and hold the element in its position.
(d) Do these steps for the sensor elements on the aft keel beam:
1) Tie the existing cord to the aft end of the new element.
2) Slowly pull the forward through the keel beam.
a) Use the access holes below the keel beam to help guide the element through
the holes in the structure.
3) Remove the cord from the element.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-024
(2) Remove the caps from the end fittings of the electrical connectors [3].
SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-025
WARNING
DO THE STEPS BELOW IF THE AIRPLANE OPERATES WHERE DEICING
FLUID THAT CONTAINS POTASSIUM FORMATE OR POTASSIUM ACETATE
IS USED. ALSO, DO THE STEPS FOR ALL AIRPLANES THAT YOU FOUND
CORROSION IN THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTORS IN THE MAIN WHEEL
WELL. THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTORS ARE IN A SYSTEM THAT IS
NECESSARY FOR SAFE FLIGHT .
(3) Examine the connector [3] at each end of the heat detector for corrosion.
(a) If corrosion is present, replace the element.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-026
(4) Put the covers on the open end of the connectors [3] with caps to prevent damage.
SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-011
(5) Install the element into each of the mounting clips [4].
(a) Install the grommet [5] around the element so that each grommet [5] is in the center of
the mounting clip [4].
26-18-02
(b) Install the grommets [5] into the mounting clips [4] so that the grommet slit is at 90±20
degrees from the clamp opening and hinge centerline except at bends where the slit is
facing outside of the nearest bend (Figure 409).
1) Make sure that the grommet slit is visible and not facing adjacent structure.
2) Make sure that the element does not contact the mounting clips [4].
3) Make sure that the mounting clips [4] and grommets [5] are attached to the element
tightly.
a) If the used sensor element is in its position as a reference, remove it.
(c) Locate element in approximate installation position and temporarily support as necessary
to maintain position.
(d) Tighten the bolt [6] to secure the mounting clip [4].
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020

1) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the
mounting clips [4].
2) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the
mounting clips [4].
3) 1.65 in. (41.91 mm) between the sensor element and duct.
(f) For the Wing Leading Edge zone, make sure that the minimum clearance is as follows:
1) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the
mounting clips [4].
2) 0.15 in. (3.81 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the
mounting clips [4].
3) 1.5 in. (38.10 mm) between the sensor element and duct.

(11) For each sensor element in the wing and lower aft body, do these steps to connect the

(6.8 N·m).
(c) If it was disconnected, connect the coupling element connector or electrical connector.
(d) Install lockwires [1] on the jamnut [2] as follows:
NOTE: Lockwire diameter must fill between 1/3 and 3/4 of the hole diameter. 0.032 in.
(0.81 mm) minimum diameter, except for lockwire. 0.020 in. (0.51 mm) diameter
can be used, if the lockwire hole is 0.045 in. (1.14 mm) diameter or smaller, or
the spacing between parts is less than 2 in. (50.80 mm) and the lockwire hole
diameter is between 0.045 in. (1.14 mm) - 0.062 in. (1.57 mm) diameter.
1) Install a MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347, on the jamnut [2] and connector [3].
2) If it is necessary, install a MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347, on the jamnut [2] and
coupling element connector or electrical connector.
3) Obtain adequate tension without overstressing the lockwire [1] (MS20995NC32
lockwire, G50347), apply the number of twists per inch of lockwire as in Table 402.

A. General

SUBTASK 26-18-02-480-001
(1) Put the overheat detection module extender box assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, or test

(a) Adjust the forks, on the Electrical / Electronic (E/E) rack, by rotating the shaft to a position
where the forks start to exert pressure on the locking lever.
1) Make sure that the lever is in vertical position when locked and the electrical
connector is getting proper contact.
2) Make sure that the plug on the rear of the overheat detection module extender box
assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, or test box assembly, C26007-2, SPL-14827,
is fully engaged with the receptacle on the airplane.

2. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check
(Figure 601, Figure 602)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. References
Reference Title
26-21-01 P/B 401 ENGINE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE -
REMOVAL/INSTALLATION
B. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-1336 Thermometer - Digital, 0-150 +/- 2 Degrees F , with a Probe,
Thermocouple or Equivalent
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left
D. Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check
NOTE: This task is for fire extinguishing bottles with pressure gauges. No action is required if a
pressure gauge is not installed.
SUBTASK 26-20-00-860-002
(1) Allow the fire bottles to reach ambient temperature.
NOTE: It may take several hours with the airplanes on the ground for the halon in the bottles
to reach ambient temperature.
SUBTASK 26-20-00-010-003
(2) Get access to the engine fire bottles that are located on the aft bulkhead in the left main
landing gear wheel well.
SUBTASK 26-20-00-970-002
WARNING
KEEP MERCURY THERMOMETERS AWAY FROM THE AIRPLANE. DO NOT
GET MERCURY IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. IF THE
THERMOMETER BREAKS, MERCURY CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(3) Use a 0-150 +/- 2 degrees F digital thermometer, STD-1336, to get the ambient air
temperature by the fire bottle.
SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-003
(4) Find the acceptable pressure range for the measured ambient air temperature in the
temperature curve (Figure 602).
26-20-00
SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-004
(5) Read the pressure gauge on the fire bottles.
(a) Make sure that the pressure on the gauge is within the acceptable pressure range.
1) If the pressure on the gauge is not within the acceptable pressure range, replace
the fire bottle with a serviceable fire bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-21-01/401).

CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS.
(a) Before squibs are touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit
Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802.
(b) Disconnect D582 and D584 to test the engine 1 circuits.
(c) Disconnect D1322 and D1324 to test the engine 2 circuits.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-020-002
WARNING
PUT A PROTECTIVE CAP ON THE SQUIBS. IF YOU DO NOT PUT A
PROTECTIVE CAP ON THE SQUIBS, THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN RELEASE ITS
CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
(3) Install a shorting plug, SPL-10040, on each squib.
26-21-00
SUBTASK 26-21-00-860-002
(4) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT

SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-009
(5) Set the toggle switch on the Fire System Test Equipment, SPL-1663 (test box), to the ON
position.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-760-001
(6) Measure the resistance (RL) of the test box with a digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793,
connected to the voltmeter jacks.
(a) Write down the value of RL in the Table 502 or, in the Table 503 as applicable for the
adapter cable.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-010
(7) Set the toggle switch on the Fire System Test Equipment, SPL-1663, to the OFF position.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-011
(8) Set the multimeter to read DC volts.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-012
(9) Connect the applicable cable assembly, SPL-10039, for the primary circuit (A) to the connector
on the Fire System Test Equipment, SPL-1663.
NOTE: The primary circuit (A) is between pins 4 and 3 of each squib connector.
NOTE: Look at the placard on the test box lid to determine the applicable adapter.
NOTE: Each squib has two circuits. The primary circuit (A) is between pins 4 and 3; the
alternate circuit (B) is between pins 6 and 7. Either circuit can fire the squib. There are
different adapter cables in the test set to test each circuit.
E. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Squib Circuit Test
SUBTASK 26-21-00-860-035
(1) Push the manual override button and pull the ENGINE 1 and ENGINE 2 fire handles on the
P8-1 panel.

(2) Connect the cable assembly, SPL-10039, for the primary circuit (A) to one of the squib
connectors.
NOTE: These connectors are listed in the Table 501.
Table 501/26-21-00-993-804 Engine Fire Bottle Squib Circuit Test Data
CONNECTOR SQUIB LOCATION ENGINE DIRECTION
D582 L (forward) bottle, squib 1A squib 1B 1 CCW

D1324 R (aft) bottle, squib 2A squib 2B 2 CW

(b) Write down the voltage shown on the multimeter as (V1) in the Table 502 or, in the
Table 503 as applicable for the adapter cable.
(c) Set the switch on the test box to the ON position.
(d) Write down the voltage shown on the multimeter as (V2) in the Table 502 or, in the
Table 503 as applicable for the adapter cable.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-730-001
(4) Release the fire handle.
(a) Make sure that the handle moves toward the center 10 degrees.
(b) Make sure that the multimeter shows 0 (± 2) volts.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-760-002
(5) Calculate the resistance Rc of the squib discharge circuit using this formula:
Rc = RL (V1/V2) - RL
(a) Make sure Rc is less than or equal to 3 ohms.
NOTE: If Rc is more than 3 ohms, the circuit can not supply enough current to fire the
squib cartridge.

SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-014

(7) Disconnect the adapter cable for the primary circuit (A) from the squib connector.

connectors listed in the Table 501 are tested.
NOTE: Each squib has two circuits. The primary circuit (A) is between pins 4 and 3; the
alternate circuit (B) is between pins 6 and 7. Either circuit can fire the squib. There are
different adapter cables in the test set to test each circuit.

Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.

SUBTASK 26-21-00-010-001
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU
OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE
DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST
REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL
PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
26-21-00
SUBTASK 26-21-00-020-005
(2) Remove the discharge tube from the discharge outlets on both engine fire bottles at positions 1
through 4.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-001
(3) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the L fire bottle discharge line at position 2.
NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa).
(a) When connecting the test equipment to the discharge lines, do not let the test equipment
hang from the discharge lines.
(b) Give sufficient support to the test equipment during the flow test.
(c) Make sure that air flows through all the nozzles on the right engine.
(d) Make sure that little or no air flows through the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-002
(4) Remove the pressure source from the L fire bottle discharge line at position 2.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-002
(5) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4.
NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa).
(a) Make sure that air flows through all the nozzles on the right engine.
(b) Make sure that little or no air flows through the L fire bottle discharge line at position 2.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-003
(6) Remove the pressure source from the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-003
(7) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the L fire bottle discharge line at position 1.
NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa).
(a) Make sure that air flows through all the nozzles on the left engine.
(b) Make sure that little or no air flows through the R fire bottle discharge line at position 3.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-004
(8) Remove the pressure source from the L fire bottle discharge line at position 1.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-004
(9) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the R fire bottle discharge line at position 3.
NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa).
(a) Make sure that air flows through all the nozzles on the left engine.
(b) Make sure that little or no air flows through the L fire bottle discharge line at position 1.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-005
(10) Remove the pressure source from the R fire bottle discharge line at position 3.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-420-004
(11) Connect the discharge tubes to the discharge outlets on both bottles positions 1 through 4.
26-21-00
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR
SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE
PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE
WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND
FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and
outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head.
1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also
acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location.
(b) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head.
NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to engine
number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to
engine number 2.
(c) Remove any unwanted compound.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF
YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.
(d) At positions 1 and 3, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb
(50.8 ±2.6 N·m).
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF
YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.
(e) At positions 2 and 4, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 360 ±18 in-lb
(40.7 ±2.0 N·m).
SUBTASK 26-21-00-010-002
WARNING

REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS WHEN YOU
CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS, INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE
TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(12) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.

SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-005

(a) When connecting the test equipment to the discharge lines, do not let the test equipment
hang from the discharge lines.

SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-001
(6) Pressurize the discharge tubing that goes from L bottle to engine 1 to a pressure of 35 ±5 psi
(241.3 ±34.5 kPa).
(a) Wait at least 3 minutes for the air pressure to stabilize.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-002
(7) Close the pressure regulator.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-820-001
(8) Turn off the pressure source.
(a) Record the stabilized air pressure.
(b) Wait 2 to 3 minutes, then check the pressure.
1) Make sure that the pressure loss during the 2 to 3 minutes waiting period is not
more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa).
2) If the pressure loss is more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa), do a check of the pressure source
connections and the plugs used to seal the fire extinguishing nozzles.
3) If they do not show any indication of leakage, do a check of all fittings, seal
surfaces, valves, and tubing in the discharge line.
a) Repair the problems that you find.
SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-003
(9) Bleed the fire extinguishing plumbing to 0 psi (0 kPa) and disconnect the test equipment from
position 1.

WARNING
DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR
SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE
PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE
WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND
FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and
outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head.
1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also
acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location.
(b) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head.
NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing that supplies extinguishant to engine
number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing that supplies extinguishant to
engine number 2.
(c) Remove any unwanted compound.
CAUTION

YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.
(d) At the position 1, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb (50.8 ±2.6 N·m).
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF
YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.

tubes and electrical harness.

6. Engine Fire Switch System Shutdown Test
(Figure 501, Figure 505, Figure 506)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) This procedure checks that the engine is isolated from the fuel, hydraulic, pneumatic and
electrical systems, and that the thrust reverser is disabled when the fire handle is pulled. It
should be performed when the engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire control panel is
replaced.
(2) This test is the same for both engines.

retract the thrust reverser. However, if the EEC is not powered, the reverse thrust lever
will be blocked by the interlock and will not move to the full reverse thrust position.
Thus, the REV light will not indicate the sleeve position.

G. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation
SUBTASK 26-21-01-940-004
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT THE DOWNLOCK PINS ARE INSTALLED IN ALL OF THE
LANDING GEAR. WITHOUT THE DOWNLOCK PINS, THE LANDING GEAR
CAN RETRACT AND CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(1) Make sure that the downlock pins are installed in the nose and main landing gear
(PAGEBLOCK 32-00-01/201).
SUBTASK 26-21-01-910-001
(2) Make sure that the airplane is grounded correctly (TASK 20-40-11-910-801).
SUBTASK 26-21-01-280-001
(3) Use a force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [1].
SUBTASK 26-21-01-410-001
(4) If the protective caps [8] are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as
follows:
(a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] is 0.00 ±0.10 lb
(0.00 ±0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate.
1) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as "Value A".
2) Make sure that protective caps [8] are installed on the squibs [7] and remove the
rest of protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [1], before weighing.
NOTE: Only the weight of the protective caps located on the squibs, supplied with
the fire extinguishing bottle, are included on the weight listed on the ID
plate.
3) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7],
protective caps [8] installed on the squibs and record as "Value B".
4) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1]
("Value A") and measured weight ("Value B") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace
the fire extinguishing bottle [1].
SUBTASK 26-21-01-960-001
(5) If the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire
extinguishing bottle [1] as follows:
(a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] is 0.00 ±0.10 lb
(0.00 ±0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate.
1) Weigh the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, including its caps and record as
"Value A".
2) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as "Value B".
3) Remove the rest of the protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [1], before
weighing.
26-21-01
4) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7],
shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168 (including shorting plugs' caps), and the
protective caps [8] supplied for the squibs [7] and record as "Value C".
NOTE: Weigh of protective caps supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle for the
squibs only are included on ID plate.
5) Find the measured weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] by subtracting "Value A"
from "Value C" and record as "Value D" (Value D = Value C - Value A).
6) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1]
("Value B") and measured weight ("Value D") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace
the fire extinguishing bottle [1].
SUBTASK 26-21-01-200-001
(6) If the previous fire extinguishing bottle [1] was discharged, do this step:
(a) Check the discharge tube for any debris.
NOTE: Pay particular attention to the discharge outlets at the engine.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-200-002
(7) Examine the check valve to make sure that the ball moves freely.
NOTE: It may be necessary to further disconnect the discharge tubes to get access to the
check valve.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-860-002
(8) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT
B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT
B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R
B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L
SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-001
(9) Do these steps to install the fire extinguishing bottle [1]:
WARNING
REMOVE THE CAPS FROM THE FILL FITTING ASSEMBLY ON THE FIRE
EXTINGUISHER BOTTLES. THE SAFETY RELEASE ON THE BOTTLE
WILL NOT OPERATE IF THE CAPS ARE ON. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
INJURY CAN OCCUR BECAUSE OF FIRE.
(a) Remove the caps from the fill fitting assembly on the fire extinguishing bottle [1].
(b) Make sure that the inboard mounting lugs and support fixture mounting surfaces are
clean.
1) Sand the support fixture mounting surfaces with 60 grit or finer wet/dry aluminum
oxide sandpaper, G51464, or 180 grit or finer silicon carbide abrasive, G51924, or
garnet 240 grit (7/0) or finer sandpaper, G51465.
2) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the washers [3],
inboard mounting lugs, and mounting surfaces of the support fixture.
(c) Apply a fay surface seal with sealant, A50110, sealant, A50155, sealant, A50231, or
sealant, A50296, to the inboard mounting surfaces.
26-21-01
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT THE SHORTING PLUGS STAY CONNECTED WHEN
THE TESTER COM-1550 OR A NON-FAIL-SAFE IGNITER TESTER IS IN
OPERATION. AN ELECTRICAL CHARGE BETWEEN THE BOTTLE WALL
AND INITIATOR PIN CAN CAUSE THE BOTTLE TO DEPLOY . IF THE
BOTTLE DEPLOYS, INJURY TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU MOVE THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE.
THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE IS HIGHLY PRESSURIZED AND
HAS AN EXPLOSIVE SQUIB AS A COMPONENT . ACCIDENTAL
DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(d) Put the fire extinguishing bottle [1] in its correct position on the support bracket.
(e) Install the bolts [2] and washers [3].
1) If it is necessary, install two washers [3] between each mounting lug and support
bracket.
(f) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the fire extinguishing bottle [1] and
support bracket (SWPM 20-20-00).
1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms) or less.
a) If the resistance is more than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms), clean the bonding
surfaces between the inboard mounting lugs and support bracket with solvent,
B00083, and retest.
(g) If the discharge head does not align with the hose or tube assembly, do this step:
1) Loosen the discharge outlet gland nut and rotate the discharge outlet so it aligns
with the tube assembly.
NOTE: Even if the discharge outlet is already aligned, the gland nut is not yet
torqued down.
(h) Tighten the gland nut to 540 in-lb (61.0 N·m) - 660 in-lb (74.6 N·m).
(i) Do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test,
TASK 26-21-00-720-801.
(j) Remove the protective caps from each of the discharge outlets.
(k) Remove the protective caps from each of the discharge tubes.
(l) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, between each gland nut, or between the gland
nut and discharge head, depending on the configuration.
WARNING
USE NITRILE GLOVES FOR SKIN PROTECTION WHEN YOU USE COR-
BAN 27L. IF IT GETS ON YOUR SKIN, IMMEDIATELY REMOVE IT WITH
WATER. IF THIS MATERIAL GETS IN YOUR EYES, IMMEDIATELY FLUSH
YOUR EYES WITH WATER. GET MEDICAL AID. THIS MATERIAL
CONTAINS FLAMMABLE AGENTS WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL.
(m) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and
outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head.
26-21-01
1) The Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are
also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location.
(n) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head.
NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishing agent to
engine number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies
extinguishing agent to engine number 2.
(o) Remove any unwanted compound.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF
YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.
(p) At positions 1 and 3, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb
(50.8 ±2.6 N·m).
NOTE: Positions 1 connects T-Fitting to engine fire extinguishing bottle 1.
NOTE: Position 3 connects Hose Assembly to engine fire extinguishing bottle 2.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF
YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE
BOTTLE CAN OCCUR.
(q) At positions 2 and 4, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 360 ±18 in-lb
(40.7 ±2.0 N·m).
NOTE: Positions 2 connects Hose Assembly to engine fire extinguishing bottle 1.
NOTE: Position 4 connects T-Fitting to engine fire extinguishing bottle 2.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-710-001
(10) Do the following steps before connecting the electrical connector [6] to the squib [7]:
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES
FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC
DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE
BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Before you touch the squib [7], do these tasks for devices that are sensitive to
electrostatic discharge:
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-700-001
WARNING
MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF
THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN
FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(11) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector
26-21-01
Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.
(b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC.
(c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes.
(d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC.
(e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors.
(f) Disconnect the multimeter.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-010
(12) Remove the protective caps [8] or shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, from the squibs [7].
SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-003
(13) Connect the electrical connector [6] to the squib [7].
NOTE: The squib pins can cause damage to the electrical connector if the pins do not
correctly engage the electrical connector.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-002
(14) Test the electrical wiring up to the pressure switch [4], do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing
Bottle Pressure Switch Circuit Test, TASK 26-21-00-730-802.
SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-005
(15) Connect the electrical connector [5] to pressure switch [4].
SUBTASK 26-21-01-210-004
(16) Do the visual check of the pressure gauge, do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle
Check, TASK 26-20-00-210-802.

2. Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Removal
(Figure 401)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR
DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE.
ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO
RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSON
AND DAMAGE EQUIPMENT .
(1) The squibs are installed on the engine fire extinguishing bottles, located in the left main landing
gear wheel well. The removal procedure is the same for each squib, but each squib has a
unique location and they are not interchangeable.
(2) This task gives instructions to remove the engine fire extinguisher bottle squib.

F . Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Removal
SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-001
(1) Disconnect the applicable electrical connector [1] from the squib [2] or squib [3].
NOTE: The squibs are not interchangeable and each squib has a unique location on the
discharge port.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-005
WARNING
DO NOT MOVE OR TOUCH THE CARTRIDGE WITHOUT A SHORTING CAP
OR FARADAY CAP INSTALLED ON THE ENDS OF THE SQUIB. THE SQUIB IS
AN EXPLOSIVE DEVICE. ACCIDENTAL OPERATION OF THE SQUIB CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
CAUTION
DO NOT PUT SHUNT PLUGS ON THE FIRE BOTTLE SQUIBS. THE SHUNT
PLUGS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SQUIB PINS.
(2) Install the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, on the squib [2] or squib [3].
NOTE: The shorting caps are attached to the fire extinguishing bottles. These shorting caps
prevent the squibs from possible activation due to static electricity.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-006
(3) Remove the lockwire between the squib [2] or squib [3] and discharge outlet.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-003
(4) Remove the squib [2] or squib [3] from the discharge head.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-004
(5) Remove and discard the packing [4].

H. Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Installation
SUBTASK 26-21-02-420-001
(1) Do these steps to install the squib [2] or squib [3] into the discharge port of the fire
extinguishing bottle:
NOTE: The squibs are not interchangeable and each squib has a unique location on the
discharge port.
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES
FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC
DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE
BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Before the squib [2] or squib [3] is touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal
Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
(b) Lubricate a new packing [4] with O-ring lubricant, D50287, or Parker Super-O-Lube
lubricant, D00652.
(c) Put the packing [4] on the squib [2] or squib [3].
(d) Install the squib [2] or squib [3] into the fire bottle.
1) Tighten the squib [2] or squib [3] to 90 in-lb (10.2 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11.3 N·m).
2) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G02392 [P05-262], between the squib [2] or squib [3]
and discharge outlet.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-700-001
WARNING
MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF
THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN
FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(2) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector
26-21-02
Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.
(b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC.
(c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes.
(d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC.
(e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors.
(f) Disconnect the multimeter.
SUBTASK 26-21-02-420-003
(3) Remove the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, from the squib [2] or squib [3].
SUBTASK 26-21-02-420-002
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE SQUIB PINS CORRECTLY ENGAGE THE
ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF NOT , DAMAGE TO THE ELECTRICAL
CONNECTOR CAN OCCUR.
(4) Connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [2] or squib [3].

(3) Set the Alternating Current (AC) meter select switch, on the P5-13 panel, to APU GEN.

SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-017
CAUTION
DO NOT TURN THE FIRE HANDLE. THE EXTINGUISHER BOTTLES WILL
DISCHARGE AND CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE IF YOU TURN THE
FIRE HANDLE.
(4) Push the manual override button and pull the APU fire handle, on the P8 panel.
NOTE: The APU will do an emergency shutdown. When the APU does an emergency
shutdown, it will not do the cool-down cycle.
(a) Make sure that the voltage and frequency read 0 after 7 seconds.
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-018
(5) Set the APU and fire protection circuits to the usual positions.
26-22-00
(a) Set the APU master switch to the OFF position.
(b) Push the APU fire handle to its usual position.
(c) Set the APU master switch to the ON position.
(d) After 30 seconds, make sure that the APU FAULT light goes off.
(e) Make sure that the APU annunciator light goes off.
(f) Push and release the APU annunciator light.
(g) Make sure that the L and R MASTER CAUTION lights go off.
NOTE: The L and R MASTER CAUTION lights stay on if the airplane finds other system
faults.
(h) Set the APU master switch to the OFF position.
F . APU P28 Remote Panel Fire Handle Generator Shutdown Test
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-031
(1) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-037
(2) Do this task: Supply APU Generator Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-815.
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-033
(3) Set the AC meter select switch, on the P5-13 panel, to APU GEN.
(a) Make sure that the voltage reads 110-120 VAC.
(b) Make sure that the frequency reads 390-410.
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-034
(4) Pull the handle, on the P28 remote APU control panel, in the right main gear wheel well.
NOTE: The APU will do an emergency shutdown. When the APU does an emergency
shutdown, it will not do the cool-down cycle.
(a) Make sure that the voltage and frequency read 0 after 7 seconds.
SUBTASK 26-22-00-860-035
(5) Set the APU and fire protection circuits to the usual positions.
(a) Set the APU master switch to the OFF position.
(b) Push the APU fire handle to its usual position.
(c) Set the APU master switch to the ON position.
(d) After 30 seconds, make sure that the APU FAULT light goes off.
(e) Make sure that the APU annunciator light goes off.
(f) Push and release the APU annunciator light.
(g) Make sure that the L and R MASTER CAUTION lights go off.
NOTE: The L and R MASTER CAUTION lights stay on if the airplane finds other system
faults.
(h) Set the APU master switch to the OFF position.

G. Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-004
(1) If the protective caps [6] are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as
follows:
(a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] is ± 0.10 lb (0.05 kg) of the
weight listed on the bottle data plate.
1) Use the force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3].
2) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as “Value A”.
3) Make sure that protective caps [6] are installed on the squibs [7] and remove the
rest of protective caps from fire extinguishing bottle [3], before weighing.
NOTE: Only the weight of the protective caps located on the squibs, supplied with
the fire extinguishing bottle, are included on the weight listed on the ID
plate.
4) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7],
protective caps [6] installed on the squibs [7] and record as “Value B”.
5) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3]
("Value A") and measured weight ("Value B") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace
the fire extinguishing bottle [3].
SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-005
(2) If the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168 are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire
extinguishing bottle [3] as follows:
(a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] is ± 0.10 lb (0.05 kg) of the
weight listed on the bottle data plate
1) Use the force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3].
2) Weigh the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, including its caps and record as
“Value A”.
3) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as “Value B”.
4) Remove the remaining protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [3] before
weighing.
5) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7],
shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, and protective caps [6] supplied for the
squibs [7] and record as “Value C”.
NOTE: Weigh of protective caps supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle for the
squibs only are included on ID plate.
6) Find the measured weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] by subtracting “Value A”
from “Value C” and record as “Value D” (Value D = Value C - Value A).
7) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3]
(“Value B”) and measured weight (“Value D”) is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace
the fire extinguishing bottle [3].
26-22-01
SUBTASK 26-22-01-860-002
(3) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2

H. APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation
SUBTASK 26-22-01-420-001
(1) Do these steps to install the fire extinguishing bottle [3]:
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU MOVE THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE.
THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE IS HIGHLY PRESSURIZED AND
HAS AN EXPLOSIVE SQUIB AS A COMPONENT . ACCIDENTAL
DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Prepare the fire extinguisher bottle lugs and the support bracket mounting surfaces for
installation.
1) Remove any old sealant from the bottle lugs and the support bracket.
(b) Use sandpaper, G50260, to sand the surface of the support bracket mounting surface
until any surface finish is removed.
NOTE: Avoid removing surface finish outside of the bonding area.
(c) Using a cotton wiper, G00034, and alcohol, B50073, clean the exposed surfaces of the
support mounting bracket and the surfaces of the bottle mounting lugs.
(d) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new wiper.
(e) Apply one of these sealants to the surface of the bottle mounting lugs:
• sealant, A50110
• sealant, A50155
• sealant, A50231
• sealant, A50296.
(f) Make sure that sealing compound is applied on the full area of the bottle lug surface.
NOTE: The thickness of the sealing compound must be 0.0005 in. (0.013 mm). If
necessary, the sealing compound thickness can be between 0.010 in.
(0.254 mm) and 0.030 in. (0.762 mm) depending on fit-up of parts.
(g) Put the fire extinguishing bottle [3] in the mounted position on the support bracket.
(h) Install the bolts [4] and washers [5].
26-22-01
1) Make sure that there is sufficient sealant coming out at each edge of the bottle lugs.
2) Make sure that sealant squeeze out is smooth and approximately 0.06 in.
(1.52 mm) along the joint.
(i) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the fire extinguishing bottle [3] lugs
and support bracket (SWPM 20-20-00).
1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.0007 ohm (0.7 milliohm) or less.
a) If the resistance is more than 0.0007 ohm (0.7 milliohm), remove the fire
extinguishing bottle [3] and repeat the previous steps to prepare the bottle
surfaces for installation.
(j) If the discharge head [11] does not align with the hose assembly [9], or if the lockwire is
not installed between the gland nuts [13], do this step:
1) Loosen the discharge outlet gland nut [13] and rotate the discharge port so it aligns
with the hose assembly [9].
(k) Torque the gland nut [13] to 540 in-lb (61 N·m) - 660 in-lb (75 N·m).
NOTE: Even if the discharge outlet is already aligned, the gland nut may not torqued
down.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the lockwire and torque the gland nut if the gland
nut is torqued, and the lockwire installed.
(l) Remove protective caps from each of the discharge outlets.
(m) Remove protective caps from each of the discharge tubes.
(n) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, between the gland nut [13] and discharge outlet
boss.
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR
SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE
PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE
WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND
FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(o) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and
outlet inner diameter where the hose assembly [9] attaches to the discharge head [11].
1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also
acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location.
(p) Connect the hose assembly [9] to the discharge head [11].
(q) Remove all unwanted compound.
(r) Torque the nut [10] to 342 in-lb (38.6 N·m) - 378 in-lb (42.7 N·m).
SUBTASK 26-22-01-710-001
(2) Do these steps to connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [7] on the fire extinguishing
bottle [3]:
26-22-01
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES
FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC
DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE
BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSON AND DAMAGE EQUIPMENT .
(a) Before touching the squib [7], do these tasks for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic
discharge:
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-002
WARNING
MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF
THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN
FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(3) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector
Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.
(b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC.
(c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes.
(d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC.
(e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors.
(f) Disconnect the multimeter.

H. APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Squib Installation
SUBTASK 26-22-02-420-001
(1) Do these steps to install the squib [3] in the fire extinguishing bottle:
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES
FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC
DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE
BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Before the squib [3] is touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit
Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
(b) Lubricate a new O-ring [5] with O-ring lubricant, D50287, or Parker Super-O-Lube
lubricant, D00652.
(c) Put the O-ring [5] on the squib [3].
(d) Install the squib [3] in the fire extinguishing bottle.
1) Tighten the squib [3] to 90 in-lb (10.2 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11.3 N·m).
2) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G02392 [P05-262], between the squib [3] and
discharge outlet.
SUBTASK 26-22-02-700-001
WARNING
MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF
THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN
FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(2) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector
Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.
26-22-02
(b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC.
(c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes.
(d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC.
(e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors.
(f) Disconnect the multimeter.
SUBTASK 26-22-02-420-004
(3) Remove the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, from the squib [3].
SUBTASK 26-22-02-420-002
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE SQUIB PINS CORRECTLY ENGAGE THE
ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF NOT , DAMAGE TO THE ELECTRICAL
CONNECTOR CAN OCCUR.
(4) Connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [3].

WARNING

SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE
PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE
WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND

AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner

SUBTASK 26-23-00-020-005
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN OR TIGHTEN THE FITTING. IF YOU
TWIST THE TUBE OR FITTING, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THEM.
(1) Remove the discharge tubes from the discharge outlets on cargo fire bottle 1.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-003
(2) Connect a 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, to the forward (yellow) cargo bottle 1
discharge line.
NOTE: Alternate pressure sources can be used. The pressure source must have an accuracy
of 1 PSIG and be capable of applying a pressure of up to 55 PSIG.
(a) If it is necessary, use equipment, SPL-1647, to connect a source to the discharge line.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-004
(3) Install the nozzle caps, SPL-1648, on the fire extinguishing nozzles in the forward cargo
compartment.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-001
(4) Pressurize the fire extinguishing plumbing to 50 psig (345 kPa) to 55 psig (379 kPa).
(a) Wait at least 3 minutes for the air pressure to stabilize.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-002
(5) Turn off the pressure source.
(a) Record the stabilized air pressure.
(b) Wait 10 minutes, then check the pressure.
1) Make sure the pressure loss during the 10 minute waiting period is not more than
5.0 psi (34.5 kPa).
SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-003
(6) Bleed the fire extinguishing plumbing to 0 psi and disconnect the pressure source.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-080-004
(7) Remove the nozzle caps, SPL-1648, from the discharge nozzles in the forward cargo
compartment.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-005
(8) Connect a 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, to the aft (blue) cargo bottle 1 discharge
line.
NOTE: Alternate pressure sources can be used. The pressure source must have an accuracy
of 1 PSIG and be capable of applying a pressure of up to 55 PSIG.
(a) If it is necessary, use equipment, SPL-1647, to connect a source to the discharge line.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-006
(9) Install the nozzle caps, SPL-1648, on the fire extinguishing nozzles in the aft cargo
compartment.

(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:

Row Col Number Name

SUBTASK 26-23-00-010-010
(2) Do this task: Forward Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Removal,
TASK 25-52-17-000-801.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-860-064
(3) Do this task: Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-801.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-020-041
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR
DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE.
ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO
RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(4) Before you touch the squib, do the procedure for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic
discharge. These are the tasks:
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802.
• ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802.
(5) Disconnect the electrical connectors from the squibs.
(a) Disconnect D12794 from bottle 1 to test the forward cargo compartment.
(b) Disconnect D12818 from bottle 2 to test the forward cargo compartment.
26-23-00
RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued)
(c) Disconnect D12796 from bottle 1 to test the aft cargo compartment.
(d) Disconnect D12820 from bottle 2 to test the aft cargo compartment.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-020-042
WARNING
DO NOT MOVE OR TOUCH THE CARTRIDGE WITHOUT A SHORTING CAP
OR FARADAY CAP INSTALLED ON THE ENDS OF THE SQUIB. THE SQUIB IS
AN EXPLOSIVE DEVICE. ACCIDENTAL OPERATION OF THE SQUIB CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
CAUTION
DO NOT PUT SHUNT PLUGS ON THE FIRE BOTTLE SQUIBS. THE SHUNT
PLUGS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SQUIB PINS.
(6) Install a shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, on the squib.
NOTE: The shorting cap may have been previously attached to the side of the fire
extinguishing bottle. The shorting cap may be used to install on the squib to prevent
possibly activation due to static electricity.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-036
(7) Connect the squib connectors to the applicable connectors on test box, SPL-1664.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-730-029
(8) Set all the switches on the test box to NO DISCHARGE.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-730-030
(9) Make sure that all the circuit breakers on the test box are closed.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-860-065
(10) Close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2
B 17 C01526 CARGO FIRE EXT 1
C 16 C01523 CARGO FIRE FWD DET B
C 17 C01522 CARGO FIRE FWD DET A
C 18 C01525 CARGO FIRE AFT DET B
C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A
SUBTASK 26-23-00-730-031
(11) Make sure that none of the circuit breakers on the test box open.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-730-032
WARNING
MAKE SURE THE AREA OF THE AFT OUTFLOW VALVE IS CLEAR OF
PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT . DURING THIS TEST THE AFT OUTFLOW
VALVE DOORS CAN START TO OPERATE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(12) Do a test of the forward cargo fire bottle squib circuit.
(a) Set the EXT 1 FWD switch on the test box to DISCHARGE.
(b) Push the FWD ARMED switch on the Cargo Fire Control panel.
26-23-00
RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued)
(c) Push and hold the DISCH switch on the Cargo Fire Control panel for approximately 3
seconds.
(d) Make sure that the EXT 1 FWD circuit breaker opens.
(e) Make sure that the other circuit breakers stay closed.
(f) Close the EXT 1 FORWARD circuit breaker.
(g) Set the EXT 1 FORWARD switch to NO DISCHARGE.
(h) Set the EXT 2 FORWARD switch S1 on the test box to DISCHARGE.
(i) Wait 60 ± 6 minutes, then make sure that the EXT 2 FORWARD circuit breaker opens.
(j) Make sure that the other circuit breakers stay closed.
(k) Close the EXT 2 FORWARD circuit breaker.
(l) Set the EXT 2 FORWARD switch S1 to NO DISCHARGE.
(m) Push the FWD ARM switch, on the Cargo Fire Control Panel, to the usual position.
SUBTASK 26-23-00-730-033
WARNING
MAKE SURE THE AREA OF THE AFT OUTFLOW VALVE IS CLEAR OF
PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT . DURING THIS TEST THE AFT OUTFLOW
VALVE DOORS CAN START TO OPERATE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(13) Do a test of the aft cargo fire bottle squib circuit.
(a) Set the EXT 1 AFT switch on the test box to DISCHARGE.
(b) Push the AFT ARMED switch on the Cargo Fire Control panel.
(c) Push and hold the DISCH switch on the Cargo Fire Control panel for approximately 3
seconds.
(d) Make sure that the EXT 1 AFT circuit breaker opens.
(e) Make sure that the other circuit breakers stay closed.
(f) Close the EXT 1 AFT circuit breaker.
(g) Set the EXT 1 AFT switch to NO DISCHARGE.
(h) Set the EXT 2 AFT switch S2 on the test box to DISCHARGE.
(i) Wait 60 ± 6 minutes, then make sure that the EXT 2 AFT circuit breaker opens.
(j) Make sure that the other circuit breakers stay closed.
(k) Close the EXT 2 AFT circuit breaker.
(l) Set the EXT 2 AFT switch S2 to NO DISCHARGE.
(m) Push the AFT ARM switch, on the Cargo Fire Control panel, to the usual position.

8. Cargo Fire Dual Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test
(Figure 502)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
26-23-00
RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued)
A. General
(1) The cargo fire extinguishing discharge line flow test makes sure there is nothing blocking the
discharge lines.
(2) There are two bottles on the airplane. The contents of bottle 1 is released when the discharge
switch is pushed. Bottle 2 is discharged 60 minutes later through a flow filter/dryer. In this test,
the discharge lines will be removed from bottle 1, and from the output of the filter/dryers. The
flow will be checked from the output of bottle 1 through the discharge nozzles and the
filter/dryer output line.

2. Discharge Head Leak Check (Method 1)
A. General
(1) This task gives instructions to do the leak check for the discharge heads of the High Rate
Discharge and Low Rate Discharge cargo fire extinguisher bottles.
(a) This method is used to connect the air source to the discharge head ports.
(2) The discharge head leak check must be done every time the cargo fire extinguisher bottle is
installed.

SUBTASK 26-23-01-020-059

WARNING

DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS
CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
(a) Before touching the squibs, do the task for handling devices that are sensitive to
electrostatic discharge (TASK 20-40-12-000-802).

(WARNING PRECEDES)
CAUTION

H. Discharge Head Leak Check
SUBTASK 26-23-01-020-060
(1) Disconnect the discharge flex tubes [12] from the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle
discharge ports.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-480-006
(2) Connect the 0-60 psig dry filtered regulated air source, STD-18519, or 0-100 PSIG nitrogen
source, STD-1115, to the discharge head.
(a) Use discharge head pressure adapter, COM-25014, or female end pressure adapter,
COM-25015, to connect the air supply hose to the FWD or AFT discharge head.
(b) If necessary, connect pressure gauge, STD-13740, between the air source and the
discharge head.
NOTE: It is necessary that the pressure gauge accurately reads a 0.5 psig change.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-006
(3) Slowly apply pressure 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa) to the discharge head.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-001
(4) With a Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, make sure that there are no leaks in the air
supply hose and the discharge head pressure adapter, COM-25014, or female end pressure
adapter, COM-25015.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-790-001
(5) Do a check for changes in air pressure.
(a) Isolate the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the cargo fire extinguisher bottle and
hold 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa).
(b) Record the initial pressure value.
(c) After a minimum of 5 minutes, record the final pressure value.
(d) Make sure that the pressure has not dropped more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa).
(e) If the pressure decreases more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa), then do the following:
1) Use Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, to check for leakage in the locations
that follow (View C, Reference Not Currently Available):
• Around the gland nut
• Around the neck of the discharge head
• Around the squib
2) Make the necessary adjustments to the discharge head or gland nut to correct the
leakage.
3) If necessary, replace the applicable internal O-ring.
4) Repeat steps to check for changes in air pressure.
26-23-01
5) If the pressure does not hold within 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa) of the initial pressure, then
replace the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-23-01/401).
SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-002
(6) Reduce the dry filtered air or nitrogen source pressure to zero.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-080-006
(7) Remove the pressure adapter and the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the fire
extinguisher bottle discharge head.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-700-014
(8) Repeat the leak check for the remaining discharge heads.
I. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-095
(1) Make sure a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, is installed between the gland nuts.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-600-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR SKIN, IN
YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS POISONOUS
AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE PROTECTION.
MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE WORK AREA. KEEP
THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND FLAMES. THE COMPOUND
CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the following locations:
• Outlets threads of the discharge port
• Outlet end of the discharge port
• Shoulder sleeve of the discharge flex tube
WARNING

CONTAINS FLAMMABLE AGENTS WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL.
(a) Use Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, as
permitted alternative compounds in this location.

3. Discharge Head Leak Check (Method 2)
A. General
(1) This task gives the alternative instructions to do the leak check for the discharge head of the
High Rate Discharge and Low Rate Discharge cargo fire extinguisher bottle.
(a) This method is used to connect the air source to the far opposite end of the discharge
flex tube, not connected to the discharge head.
NOTE: Use this method when fittings are not available to connect directly to the
discharge heads.
(2) The discharge head leak check must be done every time the cargo fire extinguisher bottle is
installed.

tube [12].

G. Put the airplane back to its usual condition.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-011
(1) Connect the discharge tube to the discharge outlet on the bottle.
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR
SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE
PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE
WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND
FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred) to the outlet threads, outlet end, and
outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head.
26-23-01
1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237 or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136 are also
acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location.
(b) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head.
NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to cargo
compartment number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies
extinguishant to cargo compartment number 2.
(c) Remove any unwanted compound.
(d) Tighten the nut on the applicable tube assembly 266 in-lb (30 N·m) to 294 in-lb (33 N·m)
for the FWD (1/2 inch) nut or 342 in-lb (39 N·m) to 378 in-lb (43 N·m) for the AFT (5/8
inch) nut.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-860-014
(2) Install the air conditioning duct that is in front of the fire extinguishing bottle if it was previously
removed.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-860-015
(3) Install the lining at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment.
SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-012
(4) Install the discharge nozzles on the discharge tubes.
(a) Install the discharge nozzle [3], spacer [2], and pan cover assembly [7] on the discharge
tube and hand tighten.
NOTE: Make sure the pan cover line up with those in the bracket. Make sure the holes in
the discharge nozzle [3] and spacer [2] line up with those in the pan cover
assembly.
(b) Tighten the nut 360 ±18 in-lb (41 ±2 N·m).
(c) Secure the discharge nozzle [3] with screws [4].
(d) Install the cargo ceiling.
(e) Position the spacer [1] and retainer [5], and secure with screws [6].

I. Cargo Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Installation
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR
DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE.
ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO
RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT MOVE OR TOUCH THE CARTRIDGE WITHOUT A SHORTING CAP
OR FARADAY CAP INSTALLED ON THE ENDS OF THE SQUIB. THE SQUIB IS
AN EXPLOSIVE DEVICE. ACCIDENTAL OPERATION OF THE SQUIB CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
SUBTASK 26-23-02-420-001
(1) Do these steps to install the discharge cartridge (squib) [1]:
(a) Before you touch the discharge cartridge (squib) [1], do this task: Power Device Cartridge
(Squib) Installation, TASK 20-10-33-400-801.
(b) For Pacific Scientific Squibs, lubricate a new o-ring [3] with O-ring lubricant, D50287.
26-23-02
(c) For Kidde squibs, lubricate a new o-ring [3] with SLUBE 884-2, D50284.
(d) Install a new o-ring [3] on the discharge cartridge (squib) [1].
(e) Install the discharge cartridge (squib) [1] in the discharge head.
(f) Tighten the discharge cartridge (squib) [1] to 90 in-lb (10 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11 N·m).
SUBTASK 26-23-02-700-001
WARNING
MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF
THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN
FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(2) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector
Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector
Engine APU Cargo
Connector Pins 3 and 4, and
6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2
(a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins.
(b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC.
(c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes.
(d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC.
(e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors.
(f) Disconnect the multimeter.
SUBTASK 26-23-02-420-006
(3) Make sure that the electrical connector is not damaged.
NOTE: The squib pins can cause damage to the electrical connector if the pins do not
correctly engage with the electrical connector.
SUBTASK 26-23-02-020-015
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIBS. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE
CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY
AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
(4) Remove the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, from the discharge cartridge (squib) [1].
SUBTASK 26-23-02-860-023
(5) Make sure that the discharge cartridge (squib) [1] pins are not bent or damaged.

outlet.

26-26-01
F . Halon Fire Extinguishers Inspection
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-001
(1) Make sure that the applicable decals, nameplates, and labels are in good condition.
(a) If the applicable decals, nameplates, and labels are in bad condition, replace the
damaged decals, nameplates, and labels.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-010
(2) Do this task: Halon Fire Extinguisher Removal, TASK 26-26-01-020-801.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-200-005
(3) If the fire extinguisher has a pressure gage installed, make sure that the fire extinguisher has
the correct pressure.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-200-003
(4) Use the force gauge, COM-373, or digital force gauge, COM-25517, to weigh the full fire
extinguisher.
NOTE: A full fire extinguisher includes the pull-out pin, pull-tite seal, hose assembly with hose
strap, or nozzle, where necessary.
(a) Compare the measured weight against the weight shown on the applicable decal,
nameplate, or label found on the fire extinguisher.
1) If you find a decreased weight, it must be less than 0.125 lb (0.057 kg) from the
given weight on the decal, nameplate, or label.
NOTE: A fire extinguisher that has a leak will have a decreased weight of more
than 0.125 lb (0.057 kg).
a) If the decreased weight is more than 0.125 lb (0.057 kg), you must replace the
fire extinguisher.
2) If it is necessary, write the weight of the fire extinguisher on the applicable decal,
nameplate, or label.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE HALON FIRE EXTINGUISHERS P/N 898052
(b) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguisher is 3.5 lb (1.59 kg) - 3.81 lb (1.73 kg).
1) If the measured weight of the fire extinguisher does not agree with the given weight,
replace the fire extinguisher.
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-002
(5) Make sure that the mounting bracket is attached correctly to the airplane.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-004
(6) Make sure that the handle lockpin [2] and pull-tite seal [1] are correctly installed on the handle.
(a) If the pull-tite seal [1] is missing or broken, replace the pull-tite seal [1] or the fire
extinguisher bottle.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-006
(7) Make sure that there is no physical damage to the extinguisher.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-008
(8) If there are other manufacturer inspection or maintenance procedures that show on the
extinguisher, do those procedures.
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-011
(9) Do this task: Halon Fire Extinguisher Installation, TASK 26-26-01-430-801.
26-26-01
SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-003
(10) Make sure that the extinguisher is installed tightly to the mounting bracket.
